## Luke 5:27–32 (id: 20987)

The call of Levi led to Jesus’ second conflict with the religious leaders, and it introduced the idea that Jesus had come to save sinners.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:27-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Tax Office (ID: `keyterm:TaxOffice`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 5:29 (id: 20989)

with Jesus as the guest of honor: Matthew introduced Jesus to his friends and colleagues to honor Jesus for calling him as a disciple. It was common in the ancient world to repay honor with honor.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 5:30 (id: 20990)

Meals were rituals of social status in the ancient world, and eating with someone meant social acceptance of that person. The Pharisees refused to eat with tax collectors and other sinners, or to enter their homes, believing that this would defile them. They expected Jesus, as a rabbi, to do the same. Jesus shattered social norms by dining with sinners and allowing sinful people to touch him ([5:29](https://ref.ly/Luke5:29); [7:39](https://ref.ly/Luke7:39); [15:1](https://ref.ly/Luke15:1)). The religious leaders severely criticized him for this and derisively called him “a friend of tax collectors and other sinners” ([7:34](https://ref.ly/Luke7:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 5:31–32 (id: 20991)

Jesus responded that, like a doctor*,* his mission was to work with sick people*,* not the healthy. Jesus was not called to minister to self\-righteous people, but to those who recognized their spiritual need for God’s grace and healing.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:31-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`)

## Luke 5:33 (id: 20992)

John the Baptist’s disciples: Most rabbis had students who learned from them and served them (see also [Acts 19:1–7](https://ref.ly/Acts19:1-Acts19:7)). • fast and pray regularly: Many pious Jews fasted two days a week ([Luke 18:12](https://ref.ly/Luke18:12)). Fasting was associated with spiritual preparation ([Esth 4:16](https://ref.ly/Esth4:16)) and repentance ([2 Sam 12:22](https://ref.ly/2Sam12:22); [Joel 1:14](https://ref.ly/Joel1:14); [Jon 3:5](https://ref.ly/Jonah3:5)). Fasting is not a means of righteousness ([Isa 58:4–5](https://ref.ly/Isa58:4-Isa58:5); [Jer 14:12](https://ref.ly/Jer14:12)) but a spiritual discipline for cultivating intimacy with God. Fasting is often linked with prayer and spiritual retreat. • Why are your disciples always eating and drinking? The point was that they did not fast regularly.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); John (ID: `person:John`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Luke 5:34–35 (id: 20993)

Do wedding guests fast? Just as it would be inappropriate for anyone to fast at a wedding celebration, it was inappropriate for people to fast while the Messiah was with them. God’s final salvation is a great wedding feast that God prepares for his people ([Isa 25:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:6-Isa25:8); [Luke 14:15–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:15-Luke14:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:34-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`)

## Luke 5:36–38 (id: 20994)

a new garment . . . new wine: New cloth shrinks when washed and so tears the old; new wine expands with fermentation and breaks brittle old wineskins. In either case, both old and new are ruined. Both illustrations make the point that the old is incompatible with the new. Jesus did not come to patch up the old covenant, but to establish a new one. The Kingdom of God brings a whole new orientation to thinking and living.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:36-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Skin-Bottle (ID: `realia:Skin-bottle`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Patch (ID: `realia:Patch`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 5:39 (id: 20995)

*The old is better*: The religious leaders were resistant to change.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`)

## Luke 6:1 (id: 21008)

*His disciples began to pick the heads of grain*: The law allowed this kind of grazing while walking through someone else’s field ([Deuteronomy 23:24–25](https://ref.ly/Deut23:24-Deut23:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`)

## Luke 6:1–11 (id: 21000)

Jesus’ conflicts with the religious leaders continued with two controversies over the Sabbath. The law of Moses required Jews to rest on the Sabbath ([Exod 20:8–11](https://ref.ly/Exod20:8-Exod20:11); [Deut 5:13–14](https://ref.ly/Deut5:13-Deut5:14)), but the Pharisees had forgotten that the real reason for the Sabbath was to benefit human beings.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:1-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Do Good (ID: `keyterm:DoGood`); David (ID: `person:David`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 6:2 (id: 21014)

by harvesting grain: The disciples were not accused of stealing grain, but of working on the Sabbath. They were breaking rules established by oral tradition.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`)

## Luke 6:3 (id: 21020)

While fleeing from Saul ([1 Sam 21](https://ref.ly/1Sam21:1-1Sam21:15)), David . . . and his companions took refuge at the sanctuary of Nob. The priest Ahimelech gave them the consecrated Bread of the Presence that only priests were lawfully allowed to eat ([Exod 25:30](https://ref.ly/Exod25:30); [Lev 24:9](https://ref.ly/Lev24:9)). Jesus’ point was that a technical violation of the law is superseded by the higher law of meeting human needs. There might also be a comparison between David and Jesus as God’s chosen and anointed kings of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); David (ID: `person:David`)

## Luke 6:5 (id: 21024)

Lord, even over the Sabbath: The saying is a play on words with a double meaning. First, *son of man* often means “human being,” so Jesus affirmed that the Sabbath was made to serve human beings. *Son of Man* was also a title for the Messiah (see [Dan 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14)). Jesus is Lord of the Sabbath because he created it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`)

## Luke 6:6–7 (id: 21025)

watched Jesus closely: Jesus now had a reputation for placing human needs above the Sabbath commands, so the religious leaders hoped to catch him working on the Sabbath. • If he healed the man’s hand: The rabbis of Jesus’ day debated whether offering medical help was justified on the Sabbath; it was generally allowed only in extreme emergencies. The deformed hand was clearly not an emergency.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 6:8 (id: 21026)

*Get up and stand among us*: Jesus did not avoid controversy. He was willing to confront the Pharisees’ hypocrisy directly.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:8


## Luke 6:9 (id: 21027)

*to do good or to do evil, to save life or to destroy it?*: This question challenged the religious leaders' intentions. They were plotting against Jesus (an evil act) because he was going to heal someone (a good act).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Do Good (ID: `keyterm:DoGood`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`)

## Luke 6:12 (id: 21001)

*He spent the night in prayer to God*: See study note on 3:21. Jesus prayed before important events and decisions such as choosing the Twelve, who would carry on his ministry after him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:12
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:21 (ID: `20934@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Luke 6:13 (id: 21002)

Disciples were students and followers; apostles were specially commissioned representatives. • The number twelve symbolically represents the twelve tribes of Israel. The apostles represented the restored people of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 6:14 (id: 21003)

*Simon*, *whom He named Peter*: The name *Peter* means "rock" or "stone" (see [Matthew 16:18](https://ref.ly/Matt16:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bartholomew (ID: `person:Bartholomew`); Andrew (ID: `person:Andrew`); James (ID: `person:James`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Philip (ID: `person:Philip`); John (ID: `person:John.2`)

## Luke 6:15 (id: 21004)

The diversity of Jesus’ apostles is striking. • Matthew, also called Levi, was the tax collector for the Romans (see [5:27](https://ref.ly/Luke5:27); [Matt 9:9](https://ref.ly/Matt9:9); [10:3](https://ref.ly/Matt10:3)). • Simon (who was called the zealot): *Zealot* probably meant that he had joined the Zealot movement, a group of Jews actively seeking to overthrow the Roman government.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.2`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Thomas (ID: `person:Thomas`); James (ID: `person:James.2`); Alphaeus (ID: `person:Alphaeus`)

## Luke 6:16 (id: 21005)

Judas (son of James) was probably also called Thaddaeus ([Matt 10:3](https://ref.ly/Matt10:3); [Mark 3:18](https://ref.ly/Mark3:18)). He was a different man than Judas Iscariot or Jude the half brother of Jesus (who wrote the New Testament book of Jude). • Judas Iscariot: *Iscariot* probably means “from Kerioth,” a village twelve miles south of Hebron.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judas (ID: `person:Judas`); James (ID: `person:James.2`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`)

## Luke 6:17 (id: 21007)

all over Judea . . . Tyre and Sidon: Tyre and Sidon were coastal cities on the Mediterranean Sea northwest of Galilee. Jesus was gaining wide popularity.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`)

## Luke 6:17–49 (id: 21006)

The main theme of the great sermon of Jesus is that those who repent and enter the kingdom of God will live in a truly righteous way.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:17-49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Do Good (ID: `keyterm:DoGood`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Condemn (ID: `keyterm:Condemn`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Beam (ID: `realia:Beam`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Storeroom (ID: `realia:Storeroom`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Acacia (ID: `flora:Acacia`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Luke 6:20–21 (id: 21009)

poor . . . hungry: Cp. [Matt 5:3](https://ref.ly/Matt5:3), [6](https://ref.ly/Matt5:6). This statement likely included physical and spiritual poverty. Poverty and suffering tend to make us more dependent on God. The Old Testament includes many promises that God will feed and comfort his people ([Pss 22:26](https://ref.ly/Ps22:26); [107:36–41](https://ref.ly/Ps107:36-Ps107:41); [126:1–6](https://ref.ly/Ps126:1-Ps126:6); [Isa 49:10–13](https://ref.ly/Isa49:10-Isa49:13); [51:3](https://ref.ly/Isa51:3); [65:13](https://ref.ly/Isa65:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 6:20–23 (id: 21010)

God blesses those who acknowledge their inadequacy and weakness and turn to God for strength. The value system of God’s Kingdom is radically different from the world’s value system, in which power and strength represent success.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:20-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 6:23 (id: 21011)

*treated the prophets in the same way*: See “The Suffering Prophet” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:23
* **Associated Articles:** The Suffering Prophet (ID: `500360@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 6:24–26 (id: 21012)

Promises of blessing for the poor and oppressed are balanced in Luke with predictions of sorrow for the rich and powerful. Those who trust in themselves will be humbled, while those who depend on God will be blessed. The Old Testament prophets often pronounced woes against nations and rulers who oppressed God’s people and rejected his sovereignty (e.g., [Isa 3:11](https://ref.ly/Isa3:11); [5:8](https://ref.ly/Isa5:8); [Jer 50:27](https://ref.ly/Jer50:27); [Zech 11:17](https://ref.ly/Zech11:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:24-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 6:27 (id: 21013)

love your enemies! People were commonly taught in the ancient world to love their family and friends and hate their enemies. Jesus announced a radical new ethic of loving even one’s enemies and overcoming evil through self\-sacrificial love for others, not through violence and hatred (cp. [Rom 12:14–21](https://ref.ly/Rom12:14-Rom12:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`)

## Luke 6:30 (id: 21015)

when things are taken away from you, don’t try to get them back: Jesus’ point is that the cycle of hatred and violence that characterizes human nature can only be broken by responding with good, not evil ([Rom 12:17](https://ref.ly/Rom12:17)). We win the world through self\-sacrificial love. Jesus was not promoting injustice, as though believers should let criminals take advantage of the weak. God established governments to punish evildoers ([Rom 13:1–5](https://ref.ly/Rom13:1-Rom13:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:30


## Luke 6:31 (id: 21016)

Do to others as you would like them to do to you: While the Golden Rule appears in some form in various religious and philosophical traditions, only in Jesus’ teaching is love for all people, including enemies, the fundamental ethic that governs all behavior.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:31


## Luke 6:34–36 (id: 21017)

The law of Moses prohibited charging interest on loans to other Israelites in order to prevent exploitation of the poor ([Exod 22:25](https://ref.ly/Exod22:25); [Lev 25:35–37](https://ref.ly/Lev25:35-Lev25:37); [Deut 23:20](https://ref.ly/Deut23:20)). Jesus further instructed his followers to lend . . . without expecting to be repaid. By doing good even to those who do evil, we act as children of the Most High and imitate our Father in heaven, who is kind to all people (cp. [Rom 5:8](https://ref.ly/Rom5:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:34-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Do Good (ID: `keyterm:DoGood`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`)

## Luke 6:37–42 (id: 21018)

Do not judge others: Jesus condemned hypocritical judgment that denigrated another person. Those who condemn others will be judged by that same standard. God requires his people to love others by warning them of the dangers of sin ([Gal 6:1](https://ref.ly/Gal6:1); [Heb 3:13](https://ref.ly/Heb3:13)). Exhortation is an act of love; judgment is an act of pride.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:37-42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beam (ID: `realia:Beam`); Condemn (ID: `keyterm:Condemn`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`)

## Luke 6:38 (id: 21019)

pressed down, shaken together: A generous grain merchant would top off the buyer’s container, press it down, shake it to make more room, and then pour until the grain overflowed into the buyer’s lap.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:38


## Luke 6:41 (id: 21021)

a log in your own: Jesus did not say that the speck in our friend’s eye is not our business, but that we must first correct our own faults so that we can see clearly enough to remove the speck. Jesus condemned hypocritical judgment; lovingly holding one another accountable regarding sin is desirable.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beam (ID: `realia:Beam`)

## Luke 6:45 (id: 21022)

What people receive and value inside themselves will shape how they live. What they keep within their thoughts and desires will come out in their actions.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Storeroom (ID: `realia:Storeroom`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 6:49 (id: 21023)

*he one who hears My words and does not act on them*: See [Ezekiel 33:31–33](https://ref.ly/Ezek33:31-Ezek33:33).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** House (ID: `realia:House`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`)

## Luke 7:1–10 (id: 21028)

This episode shows God’s message of salvation is for Gentiles as well as Jews.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`)

## Luke 7:2 (id: 21039)

a Roman officer (literally *a centurion*): A centurion was in charge of a “century,” approximately 100 soldiers. There were 60 centuries, or 6,000 soldiers, in a Roman legion.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:2


## Luke 7:3 (id: 21047)

Jewish elders were leaders in the Jewish community. With his tendency to abbreviate accounts, Matthew has the centurion approaching Jesus ([Matt 8:5–13](https://ref.ly/Matt8:5-Matt8:13)). Luke tells the full story in which Jewish elders, and later the centurion’s friends, act as intermediaries.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)

## Luke 7:5 (id: 21053)

he loves the Jewish people and even built a synagogue for us: Such patronage by wealthy Gentiles was common in Jewish communities throughout the Roman Empire. It suggests that the centurion might have been a “God\-fearer” like Cornelius ([Acts 10:2](https://ref.ly/Acts10:2)), a Gentile who worshiped the God of Israel but had not fully converted to Judaism.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 7:6 (id: 21054)

I am not worthy of such an honor: The man was probably showing sensitivity to Jewish concerns; pious Jews would not enter the home of a Gentile, since it would make them ceremonially unclean (see [Acts 10:28](https://ref.ly/Acts10:28); [11:12](https://ref.ly/Acts11:12)). The centurion might also have been acknowledging Jesus’ superior status.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`)

## Luke 7:8 (id: 21055)

the authority of my superior officers: The Roman army was renowned for its organization and discipline. The centurion recognized authority when he saw it in Jesus’ words and actions.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 7:9 (id: 21056)

I haven’t seen faith like this in all Israel! While many in Israel were unresponsive to the good news of salvation, some Gentiles believed and found salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Luke 7:11 (id: 21030)

The location of the village of Nain is uncertain. It might be the modern town of Nein, 9\.6 kilometers (6 miles) southeast of Nazareth.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nain (ID: `place:Nain`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 7:11–17 (id: 21029)

Jesus raised three people from the dead: 

* the dead man in this passage,

* Jairus’s daughter ([8:40–56](https://ref.ly/Luke8:40-Luke8:56)), and
* Lazarus ([John 11:38–44](https://ref.ly/John11:38-John11:44)).

Jesus has power over life and death (compare [1 Kings 17:17–24](https://ref.ly/1Kgs17:17-1Kgs17:24); [2 Kings 4:18–37](https://ref.ly/2Kgs4:18-2Kgs4:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:11-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nain (ID: `place:Nain`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Bier (ID: `realia:Bier`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 7:12 (id: 21031)

A funeral procession: In Judaism, a large crowd of mourners and intense wailing signified great love for the deceased. Mourners were hired in some cases. • Widows were the most vulnerable members of society, and God had special concern for them ([Exod 22:22](https://ref.ly/Exod22:22); [Deut 10:18](https://ref.ly/Deut10:18); [27:19](https://ref.ly/Deut27:19)). This widow’s only son was her means of support and hope for the future; his death was a terrible loss (see [Jer 6:26](https://ref.ly/Jer6:26); [Amos 8:10](https://ref.ly/Amos8:10); [Zech 12:10](https://ref.ly/Zech12:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 7:13–14 (id: 21032)

The word translated coffin may refer to a casket or a funeral bier. A Jew was rendered unclean by touching a dead body or the coffin or bier ([Lev 21:1](https://ref.ly/Lev21:1), [11–12](https://ref.ly/Lev21:11-Lev21:12)). As when he touched the man with leprosy ([Luke 5:13](https://ref.ly/Luke5:13)), however, Jesus was not defiled. He replaced disease and death with cleansing and life.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:13-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Bier (ID: `realia:Bier`)

## Luke 7:16 (id: 21033)

*A great prophet*: Jesus was like the Old Testament prophets Elijah and Elisha.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Luke 7:18–23 (id: 21034)

The disciples of John the Baptist: See study note on 5:33. John was now in prison ([3:19–20](https://ref.ly/Luke3:19-Luke3:20)), wondering whether Jesus was truly the Messiah, since he did not act like a powerful warrior–king who would overthrow the Romans. In response, Jesus defined his messianic role.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:18-23
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 5:33 (ID: `20992@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`)

## Luke 7:22 (id: 21035)

the blind see, the lame walk: A series of allusions to Isaiah ([Isa 26:19](https://ref.ly/Isa26:19); [29:18–19](https://ref.ly/Isa29:18-Isa29:19); [35:5–6](https://ref.ly/Isa35:5-Isa35:6); [61:1–2](https://ref.ly/Isa61:1-Isa61:2)) defines God’s salvation as the restoration of fallen creation. Jesus did not come to overthrow Roman rule over Judea (so he omits “captives . . . will be freed” from his quotation of [Isa 61:1](https://ref.ly/Isa61:1)); he came for the greater purpose of reversing the effects of sin and death.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); John (ID: `person:John`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 7:23 (id: 21036)

God blesses those who do not fall away because of me: The blessing is for those who are able to put aside their personal agendas and preconceived ideas about the Messiah and accept Jesus’ path of suffering.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`)

## Luke 7:27 (id: 21037)

The quotation is from [Mal 3:1](https://ref.ly/Mal3:1). In [Mal 4:5–6](https://ref.ly/Mal4:5-Mal4:6), this messenger is identified with the prophet Elijah. Though John denied that he was Elijah ([John 1:21–23](https://ref.ly/John1:21-John1:23)), he came in the spirit and power of Elijah ([Luke 1:17](https://ref.ly/Luke1:17)) to prepare the way for the Messiah (see [3:4–6](https://ref.ly/Luke3:4-Luke3:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Luke 7:28 (id: 21038)

Jesus praised John as the greatest person who ever lived under the old covenant. But even the least person in the Kingdom of God is greater than John. The blessings of the new covenant—free and complete forgiveness of sins, the indwelling presence of the Holy Spirit, and intimate knowledge of God—give believers in Jesus a new and greater position than even John enjoyed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); John (ID: `person:John`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 7:31–35 (id: 21040)

To what can I compare the people of this generation? Jesus compares Israel to fickle children in the marketplace who play games of make\-believe. They called John the Baptist to dance (a wedding game), but the solemn prophet refused, calling them instead to mourning and repentance for their sins. Then they called on Jesus to mourn (a funeral game), but he was the bridegroom who announced the joyful celebration banquet of the Kingdom of God, so it would not have been fitting for him to mourn (cp. [Mark 2:19–20](https://ref.ly/Mark2:19-Mark2:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:31-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); John (ID: `person:John`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Flute (ID: `realia:Flute`)

## Luke 7:33 (id: 21041)

John the Baptist didn’t spend his time eating bread or drinking wine: John lived an ascetic’s life of self\-denial, appropriate to his prophetic role of announcing God’s Kingdom, the coming judgment, and the need for repentance and cleansing.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); John (ID: `person:John`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`)

## Luke 7:35 (id: 21042)

But wisdom is shown to be right by the lives of those who follow it (or *But wisdom is justified by all her children*): In Proverbs, Wisdom is personified as a woman who calls God’s people to a life of godliness and obedience to God’s commands ([Prov 1:20–33](https://ref.ly/Prov1:20-Prov1:33); [8:1–9:6](https://ref.ly/Prov8:1-Prov9:6)). Wisdom’s children are those who live righteously, as God’s word instructs.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Luke 7:36 (id: 21044)

One of the Pharisees asked Jesus to have dinner with him: Jesus has more positive contact with the Pharisees in Luke than in the other Gospels. He was a respected teacher and healer, so it is not surprising that this Pharisee invited him to dinner. Not all Pharisees were antagonistic—some were interested in his teaching or merely curious.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

Jesus’ comment in [7:35](https://ref.ly/Luke7:35) is now illustrated by one of “wisdom’s children” (see study note on 7:35), a repentant sinner who shows gratitude for the forgiveness she has received.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:36-50

## Luke 7:37 (id: 21045)

a certain immoral woman: The implication is that she was a prostitute. • Alabaster is a soft marble—white, yellow, or red—that was often used to make expensive containers like this beautiful . . . jar.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Alabaster Jar (ID: `realia:AlabasterJar`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Myrrh (ID: `realia:Myrrh`); Myrrh (ID: `flora:Myrrh`)

## Luke 7:38 (id: 21046)

she knelt behind him at his feet: At a banquet, guests reclined around a low table with their feet extended outward. The woman could not reach Jesus’ head to anoint him, so she poured the perfume on his feet. • wiped them off with her hair: Letting down her hair in public was scandalous and could have constituted grounds for her husband to divorce her; kissing his feet connoted reverence. She discarded propriety due to her gratitude.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Myrrh (ID: `realia:Myrrh`); Myrrh (ID: `flora:Myrrh`)

## Luke 7:40 (id: 21048)

*But Jesus answered him*: See study note on 5:22.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:40
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 5:22 (ID: `20984@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.6`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 7:41 (id: 21049)

500 pieces of silver (denarii) was almost two years’ wages for a day laborer. The poor were often heavily indebted to the wealthy in first\-century Galilee.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Owe (ID: `keyterm:Owe`); Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`)

## Luke 7:43 (id: 21050)

the one for whom he canceled the larger debt: Those who had been forgiven the most responded with greater gratitude and love. The woman did her extraordinary act of sacrificial love because Jesus had forgiven her sins.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.6`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 7:44–46 (id: 21051)

Simon had neglected to show Jesus the common courtesies of hospitality. Since roads were dusty, a servant would wash the dust from a guest’s feet. Greeting a guest with a kiss on both cheeks is normal in the Middle East even today. Anointing the head with olive oil was a way to honor a respected guest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:44-46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.6`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Myrrh (ID: `realia:Myrrh`); Myrrh (ID: `flora:Myrrh`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Luke 7:47 (id: 21052)

so she has shown me much love: This is a better translation than the traditional “because she loved much.” It was not that her great love for Jesus caused him to forgive her, but that the forgiveness she had already received from Jesus prompted her love.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 8:2 (id: 21074)

Magdalene means “from Magdala,” a village north of Tiberias on the western side of the Sea of Galilee. Mary figures prominently in the burial and resurrection accounts ([24:10](https://ref.ly/Luke24:10); [Mark 15:40](https://ref.ly/Mark15:40), [47](https://ref.ly/Mark15:47); [John 19:25](https://ref.ly/John19:25); [20:1](https://ref.ly/John20:1), [18](https://ref.ly/John20:18)). Some have said that Mary Magdalene was the woman of [Luke 7:36–38](https://ref.ly/Luke7:36-Luke7:38), leading to the belief that she was a former prostitute. There is no basis for this speculation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mary Magdalene (ID: `person:Marymagdalene`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`)

## Luke 8:2–3 (id: 21064)

along with some women: Rabbis of Jesus’ day did not have women disciples, so this was unusual. Some of these women, like Joanna, were from wealthy families and contributed financially to Jesus’ ministry.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Joanna (ID: `person:Joanna`); Chuza (ID: `person:Chuza`); Susanna (ID: `person:Susanna`); Mary Magdalene (ID: `person:Marymagdalene`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`)

## Luke 8:4–15 (id: 21081)

The parable of the farmer scattering seed shows that people respond in different ways to the message of Jesus about God’s kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:4-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Acacia (ID: `flora:Acacia`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Honest (ID: `keyterm:Honest`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`)

## Luke 8:5 (id: 21092)

* *A farmer* would scatter *seed* and then plow it into the soil.
* The *seed* that fell on a walking path did not remain in the *soil*, so it was stepped on and eaten by birds.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`)

## Luke 8:6 (id: 21093)

fell among rocks: Often there was a limestone layer nine to twelve inches under the soil, so that the rain was trapped and seed sprouted quickly in the moist soil. But when the rain stopped, the sun evaporated the trapped moisture and the plant died.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:6


## Luke 8:7 (id: 21094)

fell among thorns: One type of weed looked very much like wheat as it grew, but it had larger roots and took away the moisture and nutrients that the wheat needed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Acacia (ID: `flora:Acacia`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`)

## Luke 8:8 (id: 21095)

*He who has ears to hear, let him hear*: This meant everyone who was willing to listen.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:8


## Luke 8:10 (id: 21057)

the secrets of the Kingdom of God: What was hidden in the past about God’s reign was now revealed through Jesus’ words and actions. • When they look . . . they won’t understand: This quote is from [Isa 6:9](https://ref.ly/Isa6:9) (Greek version), a passage repeatedly used in the New Testament to explain Israel’s rejection of the gospel (see also [Matt 13:14–15](https://ref.ly/Matt13:14-Matt13:15); [Mark 4:12](https://ref.ly/Mark4:12); [John 12:40](https://ref.ly/John12:40); [Acts 28:26–27](https://ref.ly/Acts28:26-Acts28:27)). In its original context, the passage refers to God’s certain judgment of Israel at the hands of the Assyrians. Israel’s sin and rebelliousness had taken them beyond the point of no return, so God blinded them in preparation for judgment. Those who rejected Jesus’ message were blind to the truth.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`)

## Luke 8:11 (id: 21058)

*the seed is the word of God*: This means Jesus is preaching the good news about God's kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`)

## Luke 8:12 (id: 21059)

Satan tries to stop those who have yet to trust in Christ from understanding the truth.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Luke 8:13 (id: 21060)

Deep roots indicate spiritual depth and maturity. The Greek word translated *testing* can also mean “temptation” or “trials.”

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Luke 8:16–17 (id: 21061)

A small clay lamp was filled with olive oil and placed in a space in the wall (an alcove). The message of Jesus gives light to those who listen, and it shows their true attitudes.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:16-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Luke 8:18 (id: 21062)

*Pay attention, therefore, to how you listen*: Compare with [Proverbs 9:9](https://ref.ly/Prov9:9).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:18


## Luke 8:19–20 (id: 21063)

Jesus had four brothers—James, Joseph, Judas, and Simon—and an unknown number of sisters ([Matt 13:55](https://ref.ly/Matt13:55); [Mark 6:3](https://ref.ly/Mark6:3)). These children were probably born to Joseph and Mary after Jesus’ virginal conception and birth. (On the other hand, some believe that Mary remained a lifelong virgin and that Joseph was therefore a widower with children from a previous marriage when he married Mary.)

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:19-20


## Luke 8:21 (id: 21065)

those who hear God’s word and obey it: Jesus was not rejecting his family but announcing that spiritual relationships—being part of God’s family—supersede family ties.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 8:22–25 (id: 21066)

Jesus’ calming of the storm demonstrated his authority over nature and strongly indicated his deity, since God is master of the seas (see [Pss 65:7](https://ref.ly/Ps65:7); [89:9](https://ref.ly/Ps89:9); [104:6–7](https://ref.ly/Ps104:6-Ps104:7); [107:23–32](https://ref.ly/Ps107:23-Ps107:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:22-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Luke 8:23 (id: 21067)

a fierce storm came down on the lake: Severe storms are common on the Sea of Galilee, which lies in a valley surrounded by high hills.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:23


## Luke 8:24 (id: 21068)

he rebuked the wind and the raging waves: As Jesus rebuked demons ([4:41](https://ref.ly/Luke4:41)) and diseases ([4:39](https://ref.ly/Luke4:39)), so now he revealed his authority by rebuking the sea (cp. [2 Sam 22:16](https://ref.ly/2Sam22:16); [Pss 18:15](https://ref.ly/Ps18:15); [104:7](https://ref.ly/Ps104:7); [106:9](https://ref.ly/Ps106:9); [Isa 50:2](https://ref.ly/Isa50:2); [Nah 1:4](https://ref.ly/Nah1:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`)

## Luke 8:26 (id: 21070)

the region of the Gerasenes: Gerasa was a city of the Decapolis, thirty miles southeast of the Sea of Galilee. Jesus deliberately went to a predominantly Gentile region and ministered there.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gerasa (ID: `place:Gerasa`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`)

## Luke 8:26–39 (id: 21069)

This exorcism story demonstrated Jesus’ authority over spiritual forces. It also contrasted the faith and devotion of the freed man with the fear and rejection of the townspeople.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:26-39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Gerasa (ID: `place:Gerasa`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`); Abyss (ID: `keyterm:Abyss`); Be Demon Possessed (ID: `keyterm:DemonPossessed`); Legion (ID: `deity:Legion`); A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Fetter (ID: `realia:Fetter`); Chain (ID: `realia:Chain`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 8:27 (id: 21071)

living in the tombs outside the town: Touching a tomb or a dead body rendered a Jew unclean, or ceremonially defiled. Jesus willingly entered this unclean place to rescue a person enslaved by Satan.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 8:28 (id: 21072)

*Jesus, Son of the Most High God*: See study notes on 4:34, 41.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:28
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 4:34 (ID: `20964@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 4:41 (ID: `20969@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 8:29 (id: 21073)

*unclean* *spirit*: The word *unclean* means not fit to be in God’s presence. An unclean spirit is a spirit that opposes God and does not belong near him (see also study note on 4:33).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:29
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 4:33 (ID: `20963@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fetter (ID: `realia:Fetter`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Chain (ID: `realia:Chain`)

## Luke 8:30 (id: 21075)

A Roman legion included about 6,000 soldiers. This number shows that the man had many demons.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Legion (ID: `deity:Legion`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 8:31 (id: 21076)

the bottomless pit: The Greek word abussos means “bottomless” and often refers to the underworld prison of evil spirits or fallen angels ([Rev 9:1–2](https://ref.ly/Rev9:1-Rev9:2), [11](https://ref.ly/Rev9:11); [11:7](https://ref.ly/Rev11:7); [17:8](https://ref.ly/Rev17:8); [20:1](https://ref.ly/Rev20:1), [3](https://ref.ly/Rev20:3); cp. [2 Pet 2:4](https://ref.ly/2Pet2:4)). The metaphor is taken from the apparently bottomless depth of the ocean (the ancients had no means for sounding the ocean depths, so to them it was bottomless).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abyss (ID: `keyterm:Abyss`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 8:32 (id: 21077)

a large herd of pigs: Pigs were unclean animals for Jews, which shows that the people of this region were Gentiles. The unclean demons were fit only for pigs.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`)

## Luke 8:35 (id: 21078)

*sitting at Jesus’ feet*: The man sat in the position of a disciple, indicating his willingness to follow Jesus (see [8:37](https://ref.ly/Luke8:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 8:37 (id: 21079)

*asked Jesus to depart from them*: The power of Jesus over spiritual forces scared the people. Losing their pigs made them angry. Their rejection contrasts with the faith of the healed man.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Gerasa (ID: `place:Gerasa`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Luke 8:39 (id: 21080)

*Return home*: The role of disciples is to proclaim to the world what Jesus has done for them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 8:40–56 (id: 21082)

These two accounts of healing show that Jesus has authority over disease and death. They also show the power of faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:40-56

## Luke 8:41 (id: 21083)

*synagogue* *leader*: The leader of the local synagogue was the officer who maintained the local synagogue and organized services (see also [8:49](https://ref.ly/Luke8:49); [13:14](https://ref.ly/Luke13:14); [Acts 13:15](https://ref.ly/Acts13:15); [18:8](https://ref.ly/Acts18:8), [17](https://ref.ly/Acts18:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:41

## Luke 8:43 (id: 21084)

with constant bleeding: Perhaps it was a menstrual disorder. The tragedy was both physical and spiritual, for such bleeding rendered the woman ceremonially unclean and thereby precluded her participation in community worship ([Lev 15:25–30](https://ref.ly/Lev15:25-Lev15:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 8:44 (id: 21085)

The fringe of his robe probably refers to the tassels that pious Jews wore on the edges of their robes (see [Num 15:38–39](https://ref.ly/Num15:38-Num15:39); [Deut 22:12](https://ref.ly/Deut22:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Tassel (ID: `realia:Tassel`)

## Luke 8:45 (id: 21086)

*Who touched Me?*: Jesus recognized the touch of faith by a needy person.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 8:48 (id: 21087)

Your faith has made you well (literally *your faith has saved you*). Go in peace: Jesus said the same thing to the woman who had anointed his feet ([7:50](https://ref.ly/Luke7:50)). The Greek word for “save” can indicate either spiritual or physical healing. Peace (cp. Hebrew shalom) refers to wholeness, not just lack of conflict (see [1:79](https://ref.ly/Luke1:79); [2:14](https://ref.ly/Luke2:14), [29](https://ref.ly/Luke2:29); [7:50](https://ref.ly/Luke7:50); [19:38](https://ref.ly/Luke19:38), [42](https://ref.ly/Luke19:42); [24:36](https://ref.ly/Luke24:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Luke 8:51 (id: 21088)

Peter, John, and James formed a close group within the disciples of Jesus (see [5:1–11](https://ref.ly/Luke5:1-Luke5:11); [9:28](https://ref.ly/Luke9:28); [Mark 14:32–33](https://ref.ly/Mark14:32-Mark14:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** James (ID: `person:James`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 8:52 (id: 21089)

The house was filled with people weeping and wailing: See study note on 7:12. • she’s only asleep: Jesus emphasized the temporary nature of the girl’s state. For believers, death is only temporary “sleep” (cp. [John 11:11](https://ref.ly/John11:11); the same term is translated as “dead/died” in [Acts 7:60](https://ref.ly/Acts7:60); [13:36](https://ref.ly/Acts13:36); [1 Cor 11:30](https://ref.ly/1Cor11:30); [15:6](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:6), [18](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:18), [20](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:20), [51](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:51); [1 Thes 4:14–15](https://ref.ly/1Thess4:14-1Thess4:15); [5:10](https://ref.ly/1Thess5:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:52
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 7:12 (ID: `21031@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 8:55 (id: 21090)

her life returned: Like the raising of the widow’s son ([7:15](https://ref.ly/Luke7:15)), the girl’s normal mortal life was restored. At the final resurrection, believers will receive immortal, glorified bodies (see study note on 24:39).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:55
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 24:39 (ID: `20911@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`)

## Luke 8:56 (id: 21091)

*not to tell anyone*: See “The Secret of the Messiah” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:56
* **Associated Articles:** The Secret of the Messiah (ID: `500351@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)


## Luke 9:1–6 (id: 21096)

Jesus now sent his twelve disciples to announce the Kingdom of God and demonstrate its authority by casting out demons and healing the sick. Jesus was training them to carry on his work after he was gone.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Staff (ID: `realia:Staff`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`)

## Luke 9:3 (id: 21121)

*Take nothing for the journey*: They were to depend on God and on the kindness of the people they served.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`); Staff (ID: `realia:Staff`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`)

## Luke 9:4 (id: 21129)

*Whatever house you enter, stay there*: They were to develop lasting relationships and be content without seeking better lodging or provisions.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 9:5 (id: 21138)

*shake the dust off your feet*: Using a symbol of rejection, they were to leave such a town to the judgment it deserved.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:5

## Luke 9:8 (id: 21140)

Jews expected that Elijah would come prior to God’s final judgment (see study note on 1:17). Some Jews expected that a prophet like Moses would come ([Deut 18:15](https://ref.ly/Deut18:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:8
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:17 (ID: `20423@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`)

## Luke 9:9 (id: 21141)

*I beheaded John*: See [Mark 6:14–29](https://ref.ly/Mark6:14-Mark6:29).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`)

## Luke 9:10 (id: 21098)

he slipped quietly away: Jesus knew that the disciples needed a time of spiritual retreat. • The town of Bethsaida was located on the northern shore of the Sea of Galilee, east of the Jordan River. It was the hometown of Peter, Andrew ([John 1:44](https://ref.ly/John1:44)), and Philip ([John 12:21](https://ref.ly/John12:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethsaida (ID: `place:Bethsaida`)

## Luke 9:10–17 (id: 21097)

The feeding of the 5,000 recalls God’s miraculous feeding of Israel with manna in the wilderness ([Exod 16](https://ref.ly/Exod16:1-Exod16:36); see also [2 Kgs 4:42–44](https://ref.ly/2Kgs4:42-2Kgs4:44)). It points forward to God’s final salvation as a great feast for all nations ([Isa 25:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:6-Isa25:8); [65:13–14](https://ref.ly/Isa65:13-Isa65:14); [Luke 14:15–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:10-17

## Luke 9:11 (id: 21099)

*He welcomed them*: Jesus wanted to go away in private with his disciples, but he had compassion on the crowds and ministered to them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 9:13 (id: 21100)

*You give them something to eat*: After their ministry of healing the sick and casting out demons, Jesus wanted the disciples to demonstrate faith in God’s ability to feed the crowds.

## Luke 9:17 (id: 21101)

*twelve basketfuls of broken pieces that were left over*: God abundantly provides for his people.

## Luke 9:18–27 (id: 21102)

Peter's confession that Jesus was the Christ (or Messiah) marked a turning point. Until then, Jesus had shown his authority as God's promised leader through miracles. Now he began to explain that the Messiah would suffer in an unexpected way. 

Jesus accepted this confession, but he made it clear that his role as Messiah would include suffering and death. He also called his disciples to follow him no matter the cost.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:18-27

## Luke 9:19 (id: 21103)

*others say Elijah*:See study note on 9:8.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:19
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 9:8 (ID: `21140@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); John (ID: `person:John`)

## Luke 9:20 (id: 21104)

*The Christ*: This phrase could be translated "the LORD's anointed," a title for the king of Israel ([1 Samuel 24:6](https://ref.ly/1Sam24:6),[10](https://ref.ly/1Sam24:10); [2 Samuel 19:21](https://ref.ly/2Sam19:21); [Psalm 2:2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:2)). David was the LORD's anointed, and so was the Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 9:21–22 (id: 21105)

*not to tell this to anyone*: Jesus did not want to inspire the people to fight against Rome. His mission was to suffer, die, and rise from the dead.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 9:22 (id: 21106)

must suffer many terrible things: This was the first of Jesus’ prophecies concerning his coming death in Jerusalem (see [9:44](https://ref.ly/Luke9:44); [17:25](https://ref.ly/Luke17:25); [18:31–34](https://ref.ly/Luke18:31-Luke18:34)). Jesus would fulfill [Isa 52:13–53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:13-Isa53:12), in which the servant of the Lord (the Messiah) became a saving sacrifice for God’s people. These events were part of God’s purpose and plan. Despite opposition from human beings and from the spiritual forces of Satan, God works through the actions of human beings to accomplish his purposes. Though wicked men plotted against Jesus and put him to death, God accomplished salvation by raising Jesus from the dead ([Luke 24:7](https://ref.ly/Luke24:7), [26–27](https://ref.ly/Luke24:26-Luke24:27), [44–47](https://ref.ly/Luke24:44-Luke24:47); [Acts 2:23–24](https://ref.ly/Acts2:23-Acts2:24); [3:18](https://ref.ly/Acts3:18); [4:28](https://ref.ly/Acts4:28)). • leading priests: Though Israel had only one high priest, the upper\-class priests were the aristocracy; they served on the Sanhedrin and held positions of power in Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 9:23 (id: 21108)

take up your cross daily: This did not simply mean carrying a heavy burden, but suffering a violent death by crucifixion. Believers must be completely willing to die to themselves and to live for God, even at the cost of their lives.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 9:23–27 (id: 21107)

If any of you wants to be my follower: After predicting his own suffering and death, Jesus taught that all who follow him must also experience death to self.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:23-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); True (ID: `keyterm:True`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 9:24 (id: 21109)

hang on to your life: Placing oneself on the throne denies Jesus. The result is to lose one’s life, mainly at the last judgment ([9:26](https://ref.ly/Luke9:26); [John 12:25](https://ref.ly/John12:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 9:26 (id: 21110)

*the Son of Man … when He comes in His glory*: See [Daniel 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14); “The Son of Man” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:26
* **Associated Articles:** The Son of Man (ID: `500342@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Luke 9:27 (id: 21111)

This difficult verse might refer to: 

1. the second coming of Christ;
2. the destruction of Jerusalem in AD 70;
3. the resurrection of Jesus; or
4. the transfiguration (when the appearance of Jesus changed and his glory was shown), which immediately follows in [9:28–36](https://ref.ly/Luke9:28-Luke9:36).

The last possibility is the most likely.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); True (ID: `keyterm:True`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 9:28 (id: 21113)

About eight days later: Mark says “after six days,” referring to full days, while Luke includes parts of days. • Peter, John, and James were the inner circle of Jesus’ disciples (see also [8:51](https://ref.ly/Luke8:51); [Mark 14:32–33](https://ref.ly/Mark14:32-Mark14:33)). • a mountain: The site of the transfiguration is traditionally regarded as Mount Tabor in southern Galilee. Some have suggested Mount Hermon, which is closer to Caesarea Philippi, where Peter’s confession ([Luke 9:18–27](https://ref.ly/Luke9:18-Luke9:27)) took place.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** James (ID: `person:James`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); John (ID: `person:John.2`)

## Luke 9:28–36 (id: 21112)

At the transfiguration, Jesus’ true glory was unveiled for three disciples to witness, confirming Peter’s confession that Jesus was the Christ. Moses and Elijah confirmed that Jesus fulfilled the Old Testament prophecies concerning the Messiah.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:28-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); James (ID: `person:James`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Luke 9:29 (id: 21114)

his clothes became dazzling white (literally *became bright as a flash of lightning*): Jesus’ glory shone through his human body (cp. [Ezek 1:4](https://ref.ly/Ezek1:4); [Dan 7:9](https://ref.ly/Dan7:9); see also [Exod 34:29](https://ref.ly/Exod34:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 9:31 (id: 21115)

his exodus from this world: The Greek word exodos means “departure.” As the exodus from Egypt was God’s great act of deliverance in the Old Testament, so Jesus’ exodus from this world—his death, resurrection, and ascension—was God’s great act of deliverance in the New Testament. Jesus would accomplish a new and greater exodus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Luke 9:33 (id: 21116)

*Let us put up three shelters*: The Greek word translated *shelter* (*skēnē*) is the same word used in the Greek Old Testament for the *tabernacle*. This was Israel's tent for worship that they moved with them as they traveled in the wilderness. It also refers to the temporary *shelters* that Jews used during the Feast of Booths ([Leviticus 23:42](https://ref.ly/Lev23:42); [Nehemiah 8:14–17](https://ref.ly/Neh8:14-Neh8:17)). Peter might have wanted to extend the event by providing a place for Jesus, Moses, and Elijah to stay.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Luke 9:34 (id: 21117)

*and enveloped them*: In the Bible, clouds often cover or hide God’s presence ([Exodus 16:10](https://ref.ly/Exod16:10); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Exod19:9); [24:16](https://ref.ly/Exod24:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 9:35 (id: 21118)

The voice from the cloud recalls God’s voice at Jesus’ baptism ([3:22](https://ref.ly/Luke3:22)). •*My Chosen One* alludes to [Isa 42:1](https://ref.ly/Isa42:1), identifying Jesus as the suffering servant of the Lord. • Listen to him is from [Deut 18:15](https://ref.ly/Deut18:15), identifying Jesus as “the prophet like Moses” (see [Acts 3:22](https://ref.ly/Acts3:22); [7:37](https://ref.ly/Acts7:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`)

## Luke 9:38 (id: 21119)

my only child: The loss of an only son was especially tragic, since this left no one to carry on the family name (cp. [7:12](https://ref.ly/Luke7:12); see [Gen 22:16](https://ref.ly/Gen22:16); [1 Kgs 17:17–24](https://ref.ly/1Kgs17:17-1Kgs17:24); [Jer 6:26](https://ref.ly/Jer6:26); [Amos 8:10](https://ref.ly/Amos8:10); [Zech 12:10](https://ref.ly/Zech12:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:38


## Luke 9:39 (id: 21120)

*A spirit keeps seizing him*: Demons sometimes cause physical sickness, such as not being able to walk or not being able to speak ([13:11](https://ref.ly/Luke13:11); [11:14](https://ref.ly/Luke11:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:39


## Luke 9:41 (id: 21122)

*O unbelieving and perverse generation!*: It is unclear whether Jesus was speaking to the disciples or to the people as a whole.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:41

## Luke 9:45 (id: 21124)

Its significance was hidden from them: The disciples were often slow to understand, partly because their eyes were blinded to the truth until after the resurrection (see study note on Mark 6:52).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:45
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 6:52 (ID: `21543@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Luke 9:46 (id: 21126)

Jesus had just predicted his own suffering and death, so the disciples’ arguing about which of them was the greatest showed astonishing pride and insensitivity.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:46


## Luke 9:46–50 (id: 21125)

The disciples did not understand that Jesus would soon die, which was now seen in their pride and selfish attitude.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:46-50

## Luke 9:47 (id: 21127)

*Jesus, knowing the thoughts of their hearts*: See [5:22](https://ref.ly/Luke5:22); [7:39–40](https://ref.ly/Luke7:39-Luke7:40).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 9:48 (id: 21128)

Anyone who welcomes a little child: To welcome means to bestow honor and to treat as a social equal. The statement is shocking, since children in Jesus’ day had no social status in the community and were viewed as their parents’ property. Jesus took the lowest and most vulnerable members of society and announced that welcoming them was equivalent to welcoming him. • on my behalf (literally *in my name*): This means “as my representative.” • Whoever is the least among you is the greatest: True leadership in Jesus’ Kingdom is achieved through sacrificial service, not the exercise of power (see also [Mark 10:42–45](https://ref.ly/Mark10:42-Mark10:45)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 9:50 (id: 21130)

Anyone who is not against you is for you: Though not one of the Twelve, the man who was casting out demons was not to be discouraged since he, too, was proclaiming the message of the Kingdom. The statement was proverbial, however, and not an absolute truth, as Jesus made the inverse statement in [11:23](https://ref.ly/Luke11:23). Wisdom is needed to apply a proverb properly in a given situation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:50

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 9:51 (id: 21132)

*Jesus resolutely set out for Jerusalem*: Even though the route was not direct, Jerusalem was his final destination.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 9:51–19.44 (id: 21131)

The journey to Jerusalem marked a new stage in the mission of Jesus as the Messiah, which would include suffering. During this time, he prepared his disciples for what was ahead, and the Jewish leaders opposed him more and more.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:51

a Samaritan village: Jews and Samaritans had a history of conflict and racial prejudice; they hated each other (see study note on 10:33). The Samaritans, who worshiped on Mount Gerizim, probably assumed that Jesus was traveling to Jerusalem to worship there.

call down fire from heaven to burn them up: Some manuscripts add *as Elijah did.* Three times, Elijah called down fire from heaven against the enemies of God ([1 Kgs 18:38](https://ref.ly/1Kgs18:38); [2 Kgs 1:1–17](https://ref.ly/2Kgs1:1-2Kgs1:17)). In their zeal, James and John wanted to do the same.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:54

As he traveled toward Jerusalem to suffer and die, Jesus explained the cost to those who wished to follow him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:57-62

## Luke 9:58 (id: 21136)

*no place to lay His head*: Any disciple of Jesus must be ready to go anywhere and to give up home and security.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:58

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`)

## Luke 9:59 (id: 21137)

let me . . . bury my father: Respect for parents was a very important value in first\-century Israel. Among other things, this meant providing them with an honorable burial. Jesus called for a commitment that took precedence over all human relationships.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:59


## Luke 9:61–62 (id: 21139)

let me say good\-bye to my family: The statement echoes Elisha’s request of Elijah ([1 Kgs 19:19–20](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:19-1Kgs19:20)). Jesus required an even more complete commitment from his disciples. • puts a hand to the plow and then looks back: The ancient farmer guided a light plow with his left hand and his oxen with the right. Looking away would turn the plow out of its path. For a believer, looking back meant placing earthly concerns ahead of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:61-62

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mattock (ID: `realia:Mattock`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 10:1–20 (id: 20455)

The Lord now chose seventy\-two: The mission of the Twelve ([9:1–6](https://ref.ly/Luke9:1-Luke9:6)) represented Jesus’ ministry to Israel; this mission represented his outreach to the Gentiles. The number 72 represents the nations of the world. [Genesis 10](https://ref.ly/Gen10:1-Gen10:32) lists 70 nations in the Hebrew text, but the Septuagint—the Greek Old Testament—lists 72\. Luke, who used the Septuagint, probably wrote 72, and then a later scribe “corrected” the text to agree with the Hebrew. The point is that the Good News is for both Jews and Gentiles.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:1-20

## Luke 10:2 (id: 20470)

The harvest refers to people who need to hear the Good News and be gathered to God’s presence (cp. [Isa 27:12](https://ref.ly/Isa27:12)). There was urgency to the task, just as crops have to be brought in at the exact time when they are ripe. More workers were needed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 10:3 (id: 20479)

lambs among wolves: Jesus’ followers are not to dominate others, but to sacrifice themselves for the benefit of others. This makes them vulnerable (see [Jer 5:6](https://ref.ly/Jer5:6); [Ezek 22:27](https://ref.ly/Ezek22:27); [Hab 1:8](https://ref.ly/Hab1:8); [Zeph 3:3](https://ref.ly/Zeph3:3)), but the Good Shepherd ([Ps 23:1](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1)) protects them from the wolves.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wolf (ID: `fauna:Wolf`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Luke 10:4 (id: 20482)

Don’t take any money with you: Cp. [9:3](https://ref.ly/Luke9:3). Jesus’ disciples were to depend on God and on the help of those whom they served. • don’t stop to greet anyone on the road: This did not mean being antisocial, but staying focused on the task Jesus had given them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`)

Korazin and Bethsaida, two of the towns in which Jesus ministered, were located on the northern shore of the Sea of Galilee. • Tyre and Sidon were Gentile cities in Phoenicia, on the Mediterranean coast north of Galilee. The prophets had pronounced messages of judgment against their wickedness ([Isa 23](https://ref.ly/Isa23:1-Isa23:18); [Ezek 28](https://ref.ly/Ezek28:1-Ezek28:26)).

## Luke 10:15 (id: 20459)

Capernaum was Jesus’ base of operations during his Galilean ministry; this town saw many of his miracles (see [4:31–41](https://ref.ly/Luke4:31-Luke4:41)). • to the place of the dead: Though Capernaum was blessed by the Messiah’s presence, it would be judged for its pride and rejection of Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`); World of the Dead (ID: `keyterm:WorldOfTheDead`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 10:18 (id: 20460)

*I saw Satan fall like lightning from heaven*:See [Revelation 12:7–12](https://ref.ly/Rev12:7-Rev12:12).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 10:19 (id: 20461)

Snakes and scorpions were symbols of danger and evil (see [Num 21:6–9](https://ref.ly/Num21:6-Num21:9); [Deut 8:15](https://ref.ly/Deut8:15); [1 Kgs 12:14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs12:14); [Isa 11:8](https://ref.ly/Isa11:8); [Ezek 2:6](https://ref.ly/Ezek2:6)). Satan is the ancient serpent who tempted Eve ([Gen 3:13–14](https://ref.ly/Gen3:13-Gen3:14); [Rom 16:20](https://ref.ly/Rom16:20); [Rev 12:9](https://ref.ly/Rev12:9); [20:2](https://ref.ly/Rev20:2)), and demonic forces are described as scorpions ([Rev 9:3–4](https://ref.ly/Rev9:3-Rev9:4)). • Nothing will injure you: This didn’t mean that Jesus’ followers should take dangerous chances. The point is that God protects his servants (e.g., [Acts 28:3–6](https://ref.ly/Acts28:3-Acts28:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Scorpion (ID: `fauna:Scorpion`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

hiding these things: God uses simple things that a child can understand to shame those who think themselves wise but are foolish in God’s eyes (see [Isa 29:14](https://ref.ly/Isa29:14); [1 Cor 1:18–31](https://ref.ly/1Cor1:18-1Cor1:31)).

This parable shows that true neighbors love even their enemies ([10:29](https://ref.ly/Luke10:29)). True faith is not based on a person’s ethnic background or religious group, but on loving God and loving others.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:30-37

## Luke 10:31 (id: 20473)

a priest: Priests were descendants of Aaron from the tribe of Levi ([Exod 28:1–3](https://ref.ly/Exod28:1-Exod28:3)). They served in the Temple and offered sacrifices to God. • passed him by: A priest or a Levite became unclean and unable to serve in the Temple if he touched a dead body. This priest was unwilling to risk ritual impurity by helping a person in desperate trouble.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:31


## Luke 10:32 (id: 20474)

A Temple assistant (literally *a Levite*): Levites were members of the tribe of Levi. They were not given a tribal homeland in Israel but were dedicated to God ([Num 3:41](https://ref.ly/Num3:41), [45](https://ref.ly/Num3:45); [8:18](https://ref.ly/Num8:18); [35:2–3](https://ref.ly/Num35:2-Num35:3); [Deut 18:1](https://ref.ly/Deut18:1); [Josh 14:3](https://ref.ly/Josh14:3)) as assistants to the priests in the service of the Temple.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`)

## Luke 10:33 (id: 20475)

a despised Samaritan: This reversal in the story must have left the listeners aghast. Jews and Samaritans hated each other (see [9:52–53](https://ref.ly/Luke9:52-Luke9:53); [17:16](https://ref.ly/Luke17:16); [John 4:4–42](https://ref.ly/John4:4-John4:42)). After the Assyrians conquered the northern kingdom of Israel, Assyrian colonists intermarried with Israelites left in Samaria; these inhabitants became known as Samaritans. After the Judeans returned from Babylon, conflict arose between the Jews and the Samaritans ([Ezra 4:1–24](https://ref.ly/Ezra4:1-Ezra4:24)), who built a rival temple on Mount Gerizim and used their own version of the Pentateuch (Genesis—Deuteronomy) as their Scripture. Hatred between the two increased dramatically when the Jewish king John Hyrcanus attacked the Samaritans and destroyed the temple on Mount Gerizim (about 128 BC). In this context of mutual animosity, no first\-century Jew would expect a despised Samaritan to help a wounded Jew. But in God’s Kingdom, a despised foreigner becomes a helping neighbor!

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Samaritans (ID: `group:Samaritan`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`)

*only one thing is necessary*: Mary has discovered it. The words of Jesus were surprising. Women were expected to serve in the home, and they were not allowed to learn from a rabbi as disciples. Jesus affirmed Mary’s desire to be his disciple.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:42

## Luke 11:4 (id: 20515)

*And lead us not into temptation*: This phrase is difficult in Greek. God does not tempt his people ([James 1:13](https://ref.ly/Jas1:13)). But testing is part of Christian growth ([James 1:2–4](https://ref.ly/Jas1:2-Jas1:4)), so this raises a question: Should believers pray to avoid testing? 

The phrase probably means “protect us when we face temptation.”

## Luke 11:8 (id: 20519)

*because of the man’s persistence* (or *in order to avoid shame,* or *so his reputation will not be damaged*): The man who refused to get up would also be shamed, because hospitality was a duty in that community. In the same way, God protects his good name by caring for his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:8


## Luke 11:11–12 (id: 20486)

*a fish … an egg*:Thesewere common foods. A scorpion can look like an egg when it curls into a ball. A snake or scorpion in place of food would be a cruel trick, giving something dangerous instead of a good meal. No loving father would do that.

## Luke 11:14–32 (id: 20488)

Jesus faced two challenges. He answered the first, an accusation that he was casting out demons by Satan’s power ([11:15](https://ref.ly/Luke11:15)), in [11:17–26](https://ref.ly/Luke11:17-Luke11:26). The second was a demand for miraculous signs ([11:16](https://ref.ly/Luke11:16)), which he answered in [11:29–32](https://ref.ly/Luke11:29-Luke11:32).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:14-32

It could also mean that both Jonah and Jesus preached God's warning to repent (see [Luke 11:32](https://ref.ly/Luke11:32)).
