## Luke 11:38 (id: 20504)

*first wash before the meal*: The Pharisees had developed elaborate washing rituals to ensure ceremonial purity (see [Mark 7:1–5](https://ref.ly/Mark7:1-Mark7:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

## Luke 11:39 (id: 20505)

The Pharisees had very detailed rules for washing cups and dishes in order to be religiously clean. These rules were about ceremonial cleansing (a special washing required by religious law).

Jesus said they cleaned only the outside of the cup. By this, he meant they carefully followed outward rules. But inside, they were still unclean. They kept sin and wrong desires in their inner lives (their thoughts and motives).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plate (ID: `realia:Plate.2`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`)

## Luke 11:41 (id: 20506)

*give as alms*:Giving to those in need is an outward action. Such actions reveal internal righteousness (a right relationship with God).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Clean (ID: `keyterm:Clean`)

## Luke 11:42 (id: 20508)

*pay tithes of mint, rue, and every herb*: The law required tithing (giving one tenth to God; see [Leviticus 27:30–33](https://ref.ly/Lev27:30-Lev27:33); [Numbers 18:21–32](https://ref.ly/Num18:21-Num18:32); [Deuteronomy 14:22–29](https://ref.ly/Deut14:22-Deut14:29); [2 Chronicles 31:5–12](https://ref.ly/2Chr31:5-2Chr31:12)). The Pharisees were careful to tithe everything correctly. But they ignored what was more important: justice and the love of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rue (ID: `flora:Rue`); Mint (ID: `flora:Mint`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

What sorrow awaits you Pharisees! Woes are the opposite of blessings (see study note on 6:24–26). * **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 11:44 (id: 20510)

hidden graves in a field: Touching a grave or a dead body rendered a Jew ceremonially unclean. Jesus accused the religious leaders of being a defiling influence rather than a purifying one. This was a strongly offensive accusation ([11:45](https://ref.ly/Luke11:45)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 11:45 (id: 20511)

an expert in religious law: The experts in religious law were closely aligned with the Pharisees and shared the same zeal for keeping the law of Moses.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Scribe (ID: `person:Scribe`)

## Luke 11:46 (id: 20512)

unbearable religious demands: This refers to the oral tradition with all its intricate details on living according to *torah* (God’s instruction in the books of Moses). The oral tradition placed a great burden upon the Jewish people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`)

## Luke 11:48 (id: 20513)

*They killed the prophets*: See [1 Kings 19:10](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:10), [14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:14); [Nehemiah 9:26](https://ref.ly/Neh9:26); [Jeremiah 2:30](https://ref.ly/Jer2:30); [26:20–24](https://ref.ly/Jer26:20-Jer26:24). Luke presents Jesus as the suffering prophet ([Luke 4:22–24](https://ref.ly/Luke4:22-Luke4:24)). The suffering prophet is a prophet sent by God who is rejected and suffers. Luke shows that Jesus fits this pattern.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`)

## Luke 11:49 (id: 20514)

The quotation is not explicitly from the Old Testament.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 11:51 (id: 20516)

from the murder of Abel to the murder of Zechariah: Abel’s murder by his brother Cain was the first murder recorded in the Old Testament ([Gen 4:8](https://ref.ly/Gen4:8)). The stoning of Zechariah, son of Jehoiada ([2 Chr 24:20–22](https://ref.ly/2Chr24:20-2Chr24:22)) was the last, since Chronicles is the last book in the Hebrew Bible.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zechariah (ID: `person:Zechariah.12`); Abel (ID: `person:Abel`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 11:52 (id: 20517)

*the key to knowledge*: See [Matthew 16:19](https://ref.ly/Matt16:19); [23:13](https://ref.ly/Matt23:13).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`)

## Luke 12:1 (id: 20527)

Yeast permeates dough until it is entirely leavened. Leaven became a symbol for the permeating power of sin ([Exod 12:14–20](https://ref.ly/Exod12:14-Exod12:20); [1 Cor 5:6](https://ref.ly/1Cor5:6)). Like yeast, the hypocrisy of the Pharisees would spread until it affected all the people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hypocrisy (ID: `keyterm:Hypocrisy`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 12:2–3 (id: 20528)

These verses are a warning against hypocrisy. All thoughts will be revealed by God, who knows all things and will judge every human being ([Job 10:4–7](https://ref.ly/Job10:4-Job10:7); [11:11](https://ref.ly/Job11:11); [Pss 11:4](https://ref.ly/Ps11:4); [33:15](https://ref.ly/Ps33:15); [139:12](https://ref.ly/Ps139:12); [Prov 15:3](https://ref.ly/Prov15:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Inner Room (ID: `realia:InnerRoom`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`)

## Luke 12:5 (id: 20553)

hell (Greek Gehenna): The Valley of Hinnom was a ravine along the southwestern side of Jerusalem. It became a metaphor for hell because Israelites had offered their children as sacrifices there ([Jer 32:35](https://ref.ly/Jer32:35)) and later used it as a place to burn garbage ([Jer 19:2](https://ref.ly/Jer19:2), [10–13](https://ref.ly/Jer19:10-Jer19:13)). Jesus’ disciples should never be afraid of enemies who can only take away their life on earth. Instead, they should fear God*,* who can give eternal punishment.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gehenna (ID: `keyterm:Gehenna`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 12:6 (id: 20554)

Each of the *two pennies* was worth about half an hour of work for a person who earned a living by working day jobs.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Assarion (ID: `realia:Assarion`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sparrow (ID: `fauna:Sparrow`)

## Luke 12:8 (id: 20555)

*will also confess him before the angels of God*: At the final judgment (see [Daniel 7:7–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:7-Dan7:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Luke 12:10 (id: 20520)

The Holy Spirit draws people to God. One who blasphemes the Holy Spirit rejects his testimony about Jesus and so rejects God. This is the unpardonable sin (see study notes on Matt 12:31–32).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:10
* **Associated Articles:** Matthew 12:31 (ID: `21719@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 12:11–12 (id: 20521)

brought to trial: Jesus predicted persecution from Jews in the synagogues and from Gentile rulers and authorities. Paul and other apostles faced both kinds of persecution throughout Acts.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Bring In (ID: `keyterm:BringIn`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 12:13 (id: 20523)

tell my brother to divide our father’s estate: In Judaism, the oldest son received a double portion of the inheritance ([Deut 21:17](https://ref.ly/Deut21:17)) and was responsible for dividing up the rest after his father’s death. This younger brother wanted his share of the estate (cp. [Luke 15:11–32](https://ref.ly/Luke15:11-Luke15:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`)

## Luke 12:13–21 (id: 20522)

This parable shows the danger of trusting in riches instead of in God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:13-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`); Barn (ID: `realia:Barn`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 12:15 (id: 20524)

*Guard yourselves against every form of greed*: See [Exodus 20:17](https://ref.ly/Exod20:17); [Deuteronomy 5:21](https://ref.ly/Deut5:21); [Job 31:24–25](https://ref.ly/Job31:24-Job31:25); [Psalm 49](https://ref.ly/Ps49:1-Ps49:20); [Ecclesiastes 2:1–11](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:1-Eccl2:11).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 12:16–20 (id: 20525)

On the surface, the rich man had a perfectly prudent plan, but the constant focus was on himself (cp. [Eccl 5:10](https://ref.ly/Eccl5:10)); he never considered giving crops to the needy. He assumed that his riches would last and only wanted a place to store them, without bringing God into the equation at all ([Jas 4:13–17](https://ref.ly/Jas4:13-Jas4:17)). His judgment was just.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:16-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Barn (ID: `realia:Barn`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 12:19–20 (id: 20526)

My friend: Literally *Soul.* The man was speaking to himself. • You fool! In Scripture, a fool is not someone with a low intelligence, but one who dishonors and disobeys God ([Ps 14:1–5](https://ref.ly/Ps14:1-Ps14:5)). • You will die (literally *Your soul will be demanded from you*): The man was satisfied with his soul, but God was not.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 12:22–34 (id: 20529)

Jesus’ warning against greed ([12:13–21](https://ref.ly/Luke12:13-Luke12:21)) is followed by teaching about living in dependence on God. Jesus’ disciples were to focus on God’s Kingdom and his purpose in this world rather than being obsessed with possessions.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:22-34

## Luke 12:24 (id: 20530)

*Consider the ravens*: Ravens were ceremonially unclean ([Leviticus 11:15](https://ref.ly/Lev11:15); [Deuteronomy 14:14](https://ref.ly/Deut14:14)). But God cared even for them ([Psalm 147:9](https://ref.ly/Ps147:9)).

## Luke 12:25 (id: 20531)

*add a single hour to his life*(literally *add a single cubit* *to his length*): Worry cannot make life any longer.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cubit (ID: `realia:Cubit.2`)

## Luke 12:27 (id: 20532)

King Solomon was one of the wealthiest kings in the world during his time (see [2 Chronicles 9:13–22](https://ref.ly/2Chr9:13-2Chr9:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wild Flower (ID: `flora:WildFlower`); Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Luke 12:30 (id: 20533)

*the Gentiles*: The Greek word *ethnē* can mean “the nations” or “the Gentiles.” To a Jewish audience, it meant all who were not God’s people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 12:31 (id: 20534)

*seek His kingdom*: Live God’s way, follow his purpose, and obey him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 12:32 (id: 20535)

*little flock*: God is the shepherd of his people ([Psalm 23](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1-Ps23:6); [Jeremiah 13:17](https://ref.ly/Jer13:17); [Ezekiel 34](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:1-Ezek34:31); [Zechariah 10:3](https://ref.ly/Zech10:3)). Israel’s leaders were also known as shepherds. The prophets criticized them for harming or neglecting their people ("flock"; [Ezekiel 34](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:1-Ezek34:31); [Zechariah 10:3](https://ref.ly/Zech10:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Luke 12:33 (id: 20536)

Sell your possessions and give to those in need: This is not a command to liquidate all personal possessions, since elsewhere in the Bible it is assumed that believers will own property. It means recognizing that everything we have is God’s and should be used to serve him and his people (see [Acts 2:44–45](https://ref.ly/Acts2:44-Acts2:45); [4:32–34](https://ref.ly/Acts4:32-Acts4:34)). Believers are responsible for meeting the needs of the poor, especially in the church ([Gal 6:10](https://ref.ly/Gal6:10)). • treasure for you in heaven: See [Luke 16:1–13](https://ref.ly/Luke16:1-Luke16:13); [Matt 6:19–21](https://ref.ly/Matt6:19-Matt6:21). When God gives wealth, he also gives a ministry to help the unfortunate.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Moth (ID: `fauna:Moth`)

## Luke 12:35 (id: 20538)

*keep your lamps burning*: This is a picture of being ready and staying watchful ([Exodus 27:20–21](https://ref.ly/Exod27:20-Exod27:21); [Leviticus 24:2](https://ref.ly/Lev24:2); [Psalm 18:28](https://ref.ly/Ps18:28); [Matthew 25:1–13](https://ref.ly/Matt25:1-Matt25:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:35


## Luke 12:35–48 (id: 20537)

Jesus compared his faithful followers to servants in a master's house. These servants were always ready for his return and did a good job managing the resources he left with them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:35-48

## Luke 12:36 (id: 20539)

The master could return from the *wedding* *banquet* at any time of day or night. The followers of Jesus should always remain faithful and prepared for his return.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:36


## Luke 12:37 (id: 20540)

he himself will seat them: In Middle Eastern culture, the master would never serve his servants! Jesus redefined the role of the master by serving his disciples, giving himself for them, and meeting all their needs (see [22:24–27](https://ref.ly/Luke22:24-Luke22:27)). • An apron was typical garb for a servant.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`)

## Luke 12:40 (id: 20541)

*the Son of Man will come*: See “The Son of Man” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:40
* **Associated Articles:** The Son of Man (ID: `500342@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)


## Luke 12:42 (id: 20542)

The faithful, sensible servant was a household manager. Wealthy homeowners appointed gifted and educated slaves as chief executive officers over their affairs.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`)

## Luke 12:44 (id: 20543)

in charge of all he owns: Although servants in the Roman world were slaves (the master’s property), they could rise to very high social status, owning property, managing the entire household, and acting with the master’s authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** True (ID: `keyterm:True`)

## Luke 12:46 (id: 20544)

he will cut the servant in pieces: Such brutal judgment was common in the ancient world. It is uncertain whether Jesus was talking about temporal punishment or eternal damnation. • and banish him with the unfaithful: This could either mean “treat him as an unbeliever” (a temporal judgment) or “send him to hell” (an eternal judgment), depending on whether Jesus was referring to discipline for a believer or condemnation of an unbeliever. The point is that an unfaithful servant faces stringent judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`)

## Luke 12:47–48 (id: 20545)

who knows . . . who does not know: Sins of ignorance receive less punishment than intentional sins ([Num 15:22–26](https://ref.ly/Num15:22-Num15:26); [Ps 19:13](https://ref.ly/Ps19:13); cp. [Luke 23:34](https://ref.ly/Luke23:34)). • When someone has been given much: Believers will be held responsible for the knowledge and resources they have been given (see [Jas 3:1](https://ref.ly/Jas3:1)). A sin in ignorance is still a sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:47-48


## Luke 12:49 (id: 20546)

I have come to set the world on fire: Jesus’ ministry radically changed the status quo, bringing judgment on the wicked and purifying the righteous (see [3:16–17](https://ref.ly/Luke3:16-Luke3:17); [9:54](https://ref.ly/Luke9:54); [17:29](https://ref.ly/Luke17:29); [Deut 4:24](https://ref.ly/Deut4:24); [Amos 5:6](https://ref.ly/Amos5:6); [Heb 12:29](https://ref.ly/Heb12:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:49


## Luke 12:50 (id: 20547)

*a baptism to undergo*: The Scriptures often describe God's judgment as an overwhelming flood ([Psalms 18:4](https://ref.ly/Ps18:4); [42:7](https://ref.ly/Ps42:7); [69:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps69:1-Ps69:2); [Isaiah 8:7–8](https://ref.ly/Isa8:7-Isa8:8); [30:27–28](https://ref.ly/Isa30:27-Isa30:28); [Jonah 2:5](https://ref.ly/Jonah2:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:50

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`)

## Luke 12:51–53 (id: 20548)

*No, I tell you, but division*: The ministry of Jesus demands a decision for or against God that divides even family members.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:51-53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`)

## Luke 12:53 (id: 20549)

Jesus quotes [Micah 7:6](https://ref.ly/Mic7:6), which was interpreted by the rabbis as the crisis and persecution that would lead to the Messiah’s coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:53


## Luke 12:54 (id: 20550)

Clouds beginning to form in the west brought rain from the Mediterranean Sea.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:54


## Luke 12:55 (id: 20551)

The hot south wind blows into Israel from the Arabian Desert to the south and east ([Job 37:17](https://ref.ly/Job37:17); [Jeremiah 4:11](https://ref.ly/Jer4:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:55


## Luke 12:58 (id: 20552)

*on your way to the magistrate*: This case involved an unpaid debt and could lead to prison for the debtor. The officer was either a court official or a prison guard. It was wise to settle matters with your accuser before reaching the court. In the same way, people should make things right with God before the final judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:58

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Set Free (ID: `keyterm:SetFree`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Luke 13:1 (id: 20564)

*the Galileans whose blood Pilate had mixed with their sacrifices*: This specific event is not mentioned in other known sources. But Pilate was a harsh governor who violently put down unrest on several occasions (see Josephus, *War* 2\.9\.2–4; *Antiquities* 18\.3\.1–2\).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`)

## Luke 13:1–4 (id: 20556)

Jesus was responding to a popular claim that bad things only happen to bad people. Sin has negative consequences, but not every bad thing is a result of sin. Jesus then clarified that all people are sinners who need to repent ([13:3](https://ref.ly/Luke13:3), [5](https://ref.ly/Luke13:5); see [Job 4:7](https://ref.ly/Job4:7); [8:4](https://ref.ly/Job8:4), [20](https://ref.ly/Job8:20); [22:5](https://ref.ly/Job22:5); see also [Pss 34:21](https://ref.ly/Ps34:21); [75:10](https://ref.ly/Ps75:10); [Prov 3:33](https://ref.ly/Prov3:33); [10:3](https://ref.ly/Prov10:3), [6–7](https://ref.ly/Prov10:6-Prov10:7), [16](https://ref.ly/Prov10:16), [24–25](https://ref.ly/Prov10:24-Prov10:25); [John 9:2–3](https://ref.ly/John9:2-John9:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Siloam (ID: `place:Siloam`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Watch-Tower (ID: `realia:Watch-tower`)

## Luke 13:4 (id: 20574)

when the tower in Siloam fell: This incident is also unknown apart from this reference. The pool of Siloam was a reservoir in the southeastern corner of Jerusalem, so the tower might have been part of the southern wall of Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Siloam (ID: `place:Siloam`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Watch-Tower (ID: `realia:Watch-tower`)

## Luke 13:6 (id: 20576)

a fig tree: Israel was sometimes portrayed as an unfruitful fig tree or a vineyard that God would judge ([Isa 5:1–7](https://ref.ly/Isa5:1-Isa5:7); [Mic 7:1–2](https://ref.ly/Mic7:1-Mic7:2); see [Jer 8:13](https://ref.ly/Jer8:13); [24:1–10](https://ref.ly/Jer24:1-Jer24:10); [Hos 9:10](https://ref.ly/Hos9:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:6

## Luke 13:6–9 (id: 20575)

The parable of the barren fig tree illustrated Jesus’ ministry to Israel. Unless the nation produced the fruit of repentance, it would face judgment. The parable is open\-ended—Israel was being offered the chance to respond. Cp.[Matt 21:18–19](https://ref.ly/Matt21:18-Matt21:19) *//* [Mark 11:12–14](https://ref.ly/Mark11:12-Mark11:14).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:6-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 13:9 (id: 20577)

cut it down: A stump or fallen tree was an image of judgment or destruction (cp. [3:9](https://ref.ly/Luke3:9); see also [Isa 6:13](https://ref.ly/Isa6:13); [10:34](https://ref.ly/Isa10:34); [11:1](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1); [Jer 46:22](https://ref.ly/Jer46:22); [Dan 4:23](https://ref.ly/Dan4:23); [Matt 3:10](https://ref.ly/Matt3:10); [7:19](https://ref.ly/Matt7:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:9


## Luke 13:10 (id: 20557)

as Jesus was teaching in a synagogue (see [4:14–30](https://ref.ly/Luke4:14-Luke4:30)): In the synagogue, visiting rabbis were often asked to teach after the Law and the Prophets were read.

## Luke 13:14 (id: 20559)

not on the Sabbath: Rabbis debated whether to give medical help on the Sabbath and decided it was acceptable only in cases of extreme emergency.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Religious Official (ID: `person:ReligiousOfficial`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 13:15–16 (id: 20560)

You hypocrites! The religious leaders would take care of their own animals on the Sabbath (to protect their investment) but then refuse to meet the needs of a fellow human being. In an ironic wordplay, the same word is translated untie ([13:15](https://ref.ly/Luke13:15)) and released ([13:16](https://ref.ly/Luke13:16)). The religious leaders would free their animals but not a daughter of Abraham—one of God’s chosen people and a recipient of his favor. • in bondage by Satan: Jesus’ exorcisms and healings manifested the Kingdom of God and the defeat of Satan (see [11:20](https://ref.ly/Luke11:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:15-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Animal Pen (ID: `realia:AnimalPen`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 13:17 (id: 20561)

Jesus gave a wise answer that made his enemies feel shame and fall silent. In Jewish society, honor was a very important value.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glorious (ID: `keyterm:Glorious`)

## Luke 13:18–21 (id: 20562)

The parables of the mustard seed and the yeast reveal the nature of the kingdom of God. Like a mustard seed, it grows from a tiny size until it becomes large. Like yeast (*leaven*) in dough, it spreads through everything.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:18-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Saton (ID: `realia:Saton`); Mustard (ID: `flora:Mustard`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`)

## Luke 13:19 (id: 20563)

*the birds of the air nested in its branches*: The birds represent people who see the kingdom as a safe and secure place.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Mustard (ID: `flora:Mustard`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`)

## Luke 13:21 (id: 20565)

*It is like the leaven*: Leaven (or yeast) can represent evil (see [12:1](https://ref.ly/Luke12:1); [1 Corinthians 5:6](https://ref.ly/1Cor5:6); [Galatians 5:9](https://ref.ly/Gal5:9)). But here it shows the positive, spreading, and transforming power of the kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Saton (ID: `realia:Saton`)

## Luke 13:23 (id: 20566)

*will only a few people be saved?*: Some believed that only a small number would be saved. Others thought that all Jews would be saved because they were descendants of Abraham (see study note on 3:8).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:23
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:8 (ID: `20946@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Luke 13:25 (id: 20567)

When the master of the house has locked the door: At a banquet, the master of the household locked the door after the invited guests had arrived. • I don’t know you: In Scripture, *knowing* often means being chosen by God for a special relationship ([Isa 63:16](https://ref.ly/Isa63:16); [Jer 1:5](https://ref.ly/Jer1:5); [Amos 3:2](https://ref.ly/Amos3:2)). The people of Israel were God’s chosen people who had descended from Abraham (where you come from), but those who failed to respond to the invitation did not have a relationship with God. The same can be said of all who fail to respond to the Good News of the Kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Door (ID: `realia:Door`)

## Luke 13:28 (id: 20568)

The expression weeping and gnashing of teeth portrays rejection and suffering. • Abraham, Isaac*,* and Jacob were the three great founding patriarchs of the nation of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`)

## Luke 13:29 (id: 20569)

While many of God’s chosen people, the Israelites, have rejected the gospel, other people . . . from all over the world will respond to God’s offer of salvation and attend the messianic banquet (see [14:15](https://ref.ly/Luke14:15); [17:20](https://ref.ly/Luke17:20); [Isa 2:2](https://ref.ly/Isa2:2); [25:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:6-Isa25:8); [51:4](https://ref.ly/Isa51:4); [52:10](https://ref.ly/Isa52:10); [55:5](https://ref.ly/Isa55:5); [56:7](https://ref.ly/Isa56:7); [59:19](https://ref.ly/Isa59:19); [Mic 4:1–2](https://ref.ly/Mic4:1-Mic4:2); [Zech 2:13](https://ref.ly/Zech2:13); [Mal 1:11](https://ref.ly/Mal1:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 13:32 (id: 20570)

Go tell that fox: Among first\-century Jews, foxes were viewed as destructive and worthless pests. • casting out demons and healing people: Jesus’ exorcisms and healings were evidence that God’s Kingdom was present; his resurrection on the third day would vindicate him and prove that he inaugurated God’s Kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`)

## Luke 13:33 (id: 20571)

*a prophet to perish*: See “The Suffering Prophet” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:33
* **Associated Articles:** The Suffering Prophet (ID: `500360@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 13:34 (id: 20572)

*as a hen gathers her chicks*: Compare [Deuteronomy 32:11](https://ref.ly/Deut32:11); [Ruth 2:12](https://ref.ly/Ruth2:12); [Psalms 17:8](https://ref.ly/Ps17:8); [36:7](https://ref.ly/Ps36:7); [57:1](https://ref.ly/Ps57:1); [61:4](https://ref.ly/Ps61:4); [63:7](https://ref.ly/Ps63:7); [91:4](https://ref.ly/Ps91:4).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`)

## Luke 13:35 (id: 20573)

your house is abandoned: Jesus predicted the destruction of Jerusalem that took place in AD 70\. • Blessings on the one who comes in the name of the Lord! This refers to [Ps 118:26](https://ref.ly/Ps118:26). [Psalm 118](https://ref.ly/Ps118:1-Ps118:29) was one of the psalms sung by pilgrims going up to Jerusalem for the annual festivals. Here Jesus referred to his second coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 14:1 (id: 20584)

*in the home of a leading Pharisee*: In the Gospel of Luke, Jesus often eats with others.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 14:2 (id: 20591)

*a man with dropsy*: Dropsy, medically known as edema, is an accumulation of fluid in tissues or a body cavity that causes swelling. It is usually a symptom of a more serious illness.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:2


## Luke 14:3 (id: 20594)

*Is it lawful to heal on the Sabbath or not?*: The Jewish rabbis (religious teachers with authority) debated this question (see study note on 6:6–7).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:3
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 6:6-7 (ID: `21025@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`)

## Luke 14:5 (id: 20595)

Jesus pointed out the hypocrisy of the Pharisees. Hypocrisy means pretending to be righteous while acting differently. See also study note on 13:15–16.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:5
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 13:15-16 (ID: `20560@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 14:7 (id: 20596)

*the places of honor*: Meals in the ancient world showed social status. A person’s place at the table depended on that status. The food each guest received could also reflect status. These guests were trying to get the places of highest honor.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`)

## Luke 14:8 (id: 20597)

The response of Jesus comments on a teaching found in [Proverbs 25:6–7](https://ref.ly/Prov25:6-Prov25:7). In that passage, wisdom warns people not to take places of honor for themselves. Instead, they should wait to be invited to a higher place. When Jesus gave his response, he applied this wisdom to his own time. He taught that people should not seek honor for themselves. Instead, they should be humble and allow God or others to honor them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:8


## Luke 14:9 (id: 20598)

*in humiliation*: In first\-century Jewish culture, honor was very important. This kind of humiliation would have been almost worse than death.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:9


## Luke 14:12–13 (id: 20578)

*do not invite your friends*: Jesus challenged the common use of banquets to show and increase social status in the community. A host would invite friends of similar status and a few people of higher status. Those honored guests were then expected to invite the host back, which could improve the host’s reputation and position. 

Jesus turned this social order upside down by telling his followers to invite people who had no status and could not repay them. In this way, God invites sinful people to share in his banquet of salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:12-13


## Luke 14:14 (id: 20579)

*the resurrection of the righteous*: See [Daniel 12:2](https://ref.ly/Dan12:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Luke 14:15–24 (id: 20580)

This parable shows what was happening in the ministry of Jesus. The rich, powerful, and important people rejected his invitation to God’s banquet of salvation, so they would be left out. Meanwhile, poor people and outcasts accepted the invitation (see also [1:52–53](https://ref.ly/Luke1:52-Luke1:53); [6:21](https://ref.ly/Luke6:21), [25](https://ref.ly/Luke6:25); [10:15](https://ref.ly/Luke10:15); [18:14](https://ref.ly/Luke18:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:15-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Interpret (ID: `keyterm:Interpret`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); A Scribe (ID: `person:Scribe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 14:17 (id: 20581)

*Come, for everything is now ready*: The invitations had been sent earlier. The guests were called when the meal was ready.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:17


## Luke 14:18 (id: 20582)

they all began making excuses: All such excuses would have been a great affront to the host, who had made a great investment in this important social event. These guests had previously accepted the invitation, and all of their excuses were weak. Clearly, they just didn’t want to attend the banquet. • I have just bought a field and must inspect it: No one would buy a field without first inspecting it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 14:19 (id: 20583)

*oxen, and I am going to try them out*: This is another weak excuse. No one would buy oxen without having seen them plow.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Interpret (ID: `keyterm:Interpret`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 14:20 (id: 20585)

I just got married: Some see this as a legitimate excuse since the Old Testament exempted men from military service in their first year of marriage ([Deut 20:7](https://ref.ly/Deut20:7); [24:5](https://ref.ly/Deut24:5)), but this feast was a local community event, not a distant war. Furthermore, in an Israelite village, a marriage and a banquet would never be planned at the same time, so there was no real conflict.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:20


## Luke 14:21 (id: 20586)

*The poor, the crippled, the blind, and the lame* were often treated as outsiders in Israel. Jesus reached out to them and helped them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:21


## Luke 14:23 (id: 20587)

*Go out to the highways and hedges*: These people invited might refer to the Gentile, who later received the good news about Jesus and the kingdom of God (compare [Acts 9:15](https://ref.ly/Acts9:15); [13:46–48](https://ref.ly/Acts13:46-Acts13:48); [18:4–6](https://ref.ly/Acts18:4-Acts18:6); [Romans 11:11–12](https://ref.ly/Rom11:11-Rom11:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 14:25–35 (id: 20588)

*large crowds were traveling with Jesus*: Jesus was very popular, but he was about to teach that the cost of following him was also very high.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:25-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`); Watch-Tower (ID: `realia:Watch-tower`)

## Luke 14:26 (id: 20589)

*If anyone comes to Me and does not hate … even his own life*: Following Jesus requires complete dedication. For the Christian, love for family and one's own life must not compete with devotion to Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 14:27 (id: 20590)

*carry his cross*: This symbolizes rejecting personal desires. A criminal sentenced to crucifixion would carry the horizontal beam of the crossto the place where the authorities would kill them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 14:34 (id: 20592)

Salt . . . if it loses its flavor: Sodium chloride cannot actually lose its saltiness. Jesus might be speaking hypothetically (if salt were to lose), or he might be referring to a combination of minerals that included salt. The salt around the Dead Sea was a mixture of sodium chloride and other minerals. When the sodium chloride was removed, what was left behind was “salt” without saltiness.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:34


## Luke 14:35 (id: 20593)

good neither for the soil nor for the manure pile: Salt had various uses in the ancient world, including flavoring ([14:34](https://ref.ly/Luke14:34)), preserving, and weed\-killing. The sludge left after removing the sodium chloride was useless.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:35


## Luke 15:1 (id: 20604)

People greatly disliked *tax collectors* because they worked for the hated Roman authorities. The tax collectors had a reputation for not being honest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`)

## Luke 15:1–32 (id: 20599)

Chapter 15 contains three related parables of things lost and found: a sheep ([15:1–7](https://ref.ly/Luke15:1-Luke15:7)), a coin ([15:8–10](https://ref.ly/Luke15:8-Luke15:10)), and a son ([15:11–32](https://ref.ly/Luke15:11-Luke15:32)). The loss of something loved causes deep sorrow, whereas finding it brings great joy. There is great rejoicing in heaven when lost sinners return to their heavenly Father.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:1-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Hired Worker (ID: `keyterm:HiredWorker`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Drachma (ID: `realia:Drachma`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Be Enslaved (ID: `keyterm:Serve.2`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kid (ID: `fauna:Kid`); Carob (ID: `flora:Carob`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Seal (ID: `realia:Seal`)

## Luke 15:2 (id: 20610)

In their self\-righteous hard\-heartedness, the Pharisees and teachers of religious law did not care about lost people. Jesus embodied the heart of God, who longs for his wayward children to return.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

## Luke 15:4 (id: 20613)

A flock of a hundred sheep was of average size for a shepherd of modest means. God’s people are often identified as the Lord’s flock ([Ps 23](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1-Ps23:6); [Isa 53:6](https://ref.ly/Isa53:6); [Jer 13:17](https://ref.ly/Jer13:17); [Ezek 34](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:1-Ezek34:31); [Zech 10:3](https://ref.ly/Zech10:3)). • leave the ninety\-nine others: Other shepherds could watch the ninety\-nine, so those left behind were not in danger. The shepherd would do anything to find the one that was lost.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Luke 15:8 (id: 20614)

ten silver coins: These coins might have been part of the woman’s dowry. • light a lamp and sweep the entire house: Palestinian homes were poorly lit and normally had dirt or stone floors. Finding a small coin could be difficult.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Drachma (ID: `realia:Drachma`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 15:11–32 (id: 20600)

Like the previous two parables ([15:3–7](https://ref.ly/Luke15:3-Luke15:7), [8–10](https://ref.ly/Luke15:8-Luke15:10)), the parable of the lost son demonstrates God’s love for the lost and the joy he experiences when they return. It also allegorizes Jesus’ ministry. The father represents God, the younger brother represents the tax collectors and sinners to whom Jesus ministered, and the older brother represents the religious leaders.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:11-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Hired Worker (ID: `keyterm:HiredWorker`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Be Enslaved (ID: `keyterm:Serve.2`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Kid (ID: `fauna:Kid`); Carob (ID: `flora:Carob`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Seal (ID: `realia:Seal`)

## Luke 15:12 (id: 20601)

I want my share of your estate now: This appalling request essentially says, “To me, you are dead.” There could be no greater insult to a father.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`)

## Luke 15:13 (id: 20602)

he wasted all his money in wild living: Jews considered the loss of family property to Gentiles in a distant land to be particularly disgraceful and grounds for excommunication (see also [Deut 21:18–21](https://ref.ly/Deut21:18-Deut21:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 15:15 (id: 20603)

Pigs were ceremonially unclean animals ([Leviticus 11:7](https://ref.ly/Lev11:7); [Deuteronomy 14:8](https://ref.ly/Deut14:8)). So this was the most shameful job a Jew could have.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`)

## Luke 15:20 (id: 20605)

* *his father saw him*: The father was watching the road, hoping his son would return.
* *He ran to his son*: Since running was not appropriate for the leader of the family, this expressed how happy the father was that his son came back.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`)

## Luke 15:21 (id: 20606)

The son delivered the speech he had practiced. Some early Greek manuscripts add *Please take me on as a hired servant.* The absence of this sentence in the majority of the manuscripts suggests his father interrupted him before he could finish what he wanted to say.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 15:22 (id: 20607)

The finest robe belonged to the father; it affirmed the son’s role as an heir and a cherished member of the household. • Get a ring for his finger: A ring was a symbol of authority. Like the robe, it indicated his status as son and heir.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Seal (ID: `realia:Seal`)

## Luke 15:23 (id: 20608)

The father had been preparing a calf for a feast. He welcomed his son with the kind of honor shown to an important guest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 15:28 (id: 20609)

The older brother was angry that his father would forgive such a sinner when he, the faithful son, had worked hard to achieve his position. Like the religious leaders, he refused to rejoice when his brother was found.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 15:30 (id: 20611)

*this son of yours*: He refused to acknowledge his own relationship to his brother.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 15:31 (id: 20612)

*you are always with me*: The parable ends without telling us how the older brother responded. In the same way, the religious leaders still had a chance to respond to the offer of the kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:31


## Luke 16:1 (id: 20622)

*There was a rich man whose manager*: Wealthy landowners commonly put managers over their estates.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 16:2 (id: 20628)

*Turn in an account of your management*: This financial statement was probably meant for the person who would replace the manager.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Stewardship (ID: `keyterm:Stewardship`)

## Luke 16:3 (id: 20630)

*too ashamed to beg*: The Jewish wisdom writer Sirach said, “It is better to die than to beg” (*Sirach* 40:28\). This reflects how many Jews viewed begging.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Stewardship (ID: `keyterm:Stewardship`)

## Luke 16:5–7 (id: 20631)

The manager called in his master's debtors and reduced their debts. He made friends who would help him when he no longer had a job.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kor (ID: `realia:Kor.2`); Owe (ID: `keyterm:Owe`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Luke 16:6 (id: 20632)

*A hundred measures of olive oil … fifty*: The measure in Greek is a *batos* (from the Hebrew *bath*), a standard unit for measuring liquids. A hundred measures could have been about 3,000 liters (800 gallons), and fifty measures about 1,500 liters (400 gallons). The exact sizes are uncertain.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kor (ID: `realia:Kor.2`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Luke 16:7 (id: 20633)

*A hundred measures … eighty*: The Hebrew word for *measures* here is *cor*. This was the standard dry measure. A hundred measures could have been around 35,000 liters (9,300 gallons or 1,000 bushels) and eighty measures around 28,000 liters (7,400 gallons or 800 bushels). The exact size of the cor is uncertain.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kor (ID: `realia:Kor.2`); Owe (ID: `keyterm:Owe`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 16:8 (id: 20635)

The ways of the world are the opposite of God’s ways. The children of this world use all their resources to get ahead in this world. God’s people do just the opposite ([16:9](https://ref.ly/Luke16:9)). • The children of the light are the people of God (see [John 12:36](https://ref.ly/John12:36); [1 Thes 5:5](https://ref.ly/1Thess5:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Unjust Deed (ID: `keyterm:UnjustDeed`)

## Luke 16:8–9 (id: 20634)

Jesus seems to praise the manager for acting wisely, not for being dishonest. It likely means that believers should use their resources wisely as they prepare for eternity.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Unjust Deed (ID: `keyterm:UnjustDeed`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Luke 16:9 (id: 20636)

worldly resources: This is a better translation than the traditional “mammon of unrighteousness” (KJV). Believers are to use their resources wisely for spiritual benefit. • they will welcome you to an eternal home: *They* are probably the friends. Just as the manager’s friends would give him security, the friends we win to the Kingdom will warmly welcome us for eternity.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Unjust Deed (ID: `keyterm:UnjustDeed`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Luke 16:13 (id: 20615)

*No servant can serve two masters*: Complete love and devotion can be given only to one master.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Be Enslaved (ID: `keyterm:Serve.2`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`)

## Luke 16:14 (id: 20616)

*The Pharisees* … *heard all of this and were scoffing*: People often thought wealth was a reward from God, so the Pharisees rejected the warning Jesus spoke about money.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Loving Wealth (ID: `keyterm:LovingWealth`)

## Luke 16:16 (id: 20617)

John the Baptist was a transitional figure—the last in the line of Old Testament prophets and the herald of the age of salvation (see [1:5–25](https://ref.ly/Luke1:5-Luke1:25), [57–80](https://ref.ly/Luke1:57-Luke1:80); [3:1–20](https://ref.ly/Luke3:1-Luke3:20); [7:18–35](https://ref.ly/Luke7:18-Luke7:35)). • everyone is eager to get in: The verb normally means “to suffer violence” and may have the negative sense of violent force into or against the Kingdom (see [Matt 11:12](https://ref.ly/Matt11:12)). More likely, it carries here a positive sense of urgency to enter the Kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`); John (ID: `person:John`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 16:17 (id: 20618)

that doesn’t mean that the law has lost its force: Although the new covenant fulfilled the Old Testament law and prophets, the law has not passed away (however, cp. [Acts 15:23–29](https://ref.ly/Acts15:23-Acts15:29)). • Heaven and earth will be destroyed and replaced by a new heaven and earth at the end of the age ([Rev 21:1](https://ref.ly/Rev21:1)), but God’s word endures forever ([Ps 119:89](https://ref.ly/Ps119:89), [160](https://ref.ly/Ps119:160); [Isa 40:8](https://ref.ly/Isa40:8); [55:10–11](https://ref.ly/Isa55:10-Isa55:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 16:18 (id: 20619)

This example fit the context by showing that Jesus’ authoritative words interpreted and fulfilled the Old Testament law. • a man who divorces his wife and marries someone else commits adultery: The Old Testament allowed for divorce but did not sanction it ([Deut 24:1–4](https://ref.ly/Deut24:1-Deut24:4)), and the rabbis of Jesus’ day debated what constituted legitimate grounds. The school of Shammai allowed a man to divorce his wife only because of unfaithfulness, while the school of Hillel allowed divorce for almost any reason. Against such a casual attitude, Jesus said that breaking the marriage vow by divorce and marrying someone else was equivalent to adultery. Elsewhere ([Matt 5:32](https://ref.ly/Matt5:32); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Matt19:9)), Jesus provided a clear exception for cases of unfaithfulness.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`)

## Luke 16:19 (id: 20621)

*Purple* was the color worn by royal people. Phoenician purple was an expensive dye made from mollusks.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`)

## Luke 16:19–31 (id: 20620)

This parable repeats the warning about the danger of riches ([16:13–15](https://ref.ly/Luke16:13-Luke16:15)). It also returns to the idea that when God’s Kingdom comes, many situations will be reversed (changed to the opposite).

The rich, proud, and powerful will be brought low, while the poor, humble, and oppressed will be lifted up.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:19-31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Lazarus (ID: `person:Lazarus`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); World of the Dead (ID: `keyterm:WorldOfTheDead`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Encourage (ID: `keyterm:Encourage`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Declare (ID: `keyterm:Declare`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Dog (ID: `fauna:Dog`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Luke 16:21 (id: 20623)

*Even the dogs came and licked his sores*: This is an image of misery and poverty. In Jewish culture, dogs were seen as unclean animals and scavengers (animals that eat what others leave behind).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dog (ID: `fauna:Dog`)

## Luke 16:22 (id: 20624)

*carried by the angels to Abraham’s side*: The Greek suggests a banquet at which guests reclined around a low table (see [John 13:23](https://ref.ly/John13:23)). Lazarus was taken to the messianic banquet in the kingdom of God (see [Luke 14:1–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Luke 16:23 (id: 20625)

*Hades*: This can refer to either the place where all people go when they die, or where the wicked go after death, as in this context.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lazarus (ID: `person:Lazarus`); World of the Dead (ID: `keyterm:WorldOfTheDead`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Luke 16:24 (id: 20626)

I am in anguish in these flames: See [Matt 25:41](https://ref.ly/Matt25:41); [Rev 20:10](https://ref.ly/Rev20:10), [14–15](https://ref.ly/Rev20:14-Rev20:15). • Send Lazarus: Ironically, the man was still treating Lazarus as a lowly servant.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lazarus (ID: `person:Lazarus`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`)

## Luke 16:26 (id: 20627)

*nor can anyone cross from there to us*: This verse gives the general principle that a person's state after death is permanent.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:26


## Luke 16:31 (id: 20629)

*they will not be persuaded even if someone rises from the dead*: Wicked people refuse to repent even when faced with overwhelming evidence of the truth.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`)

## Luke 17:1 (id: 20643)

what sorrow awaits: Traditionally rendered *woe!* (see study notes on 6:24–26; 11:42–52). • the person who does the tempting: Leaders bear a greater responsibility because they are accountable not only for themselves, but for those they lead (see [Jas 3:1](https://ref.ly/Jas3:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:1
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 11:42-52 (ID: `20507@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 6:24-26 (ID: `21012@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Luke 17:2 (id: 20650)

A millstone is a large round stone with a hole in its center, used to grind grain in a mill. A millstone could weigh hundreds of pounds.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cause to Stumble (ID: `keyterm:CauseToStumble`); Hand-Mill (ID: `realia:Hand-mill`)

## Luke 17:4 (id: 20654)

Even if that person wrongs you seven times a day: *Seven* is not intended to convey an exact number, but means “many times” (see [Ps 119:164](https://ref.ly/Ps119:164)). See [Matt 18:21–22](https://ref.ly/Matt18:21-Matt18:22).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 17:6 (id: 20655)

A mustard seed was proverbial for something very small (see [13:19](https://ref.ly/Luke13:19)). • you could say to this mulberry tree, ‘May you be uprooted’: Black mulberry trees can live for hundreds of years and have a vast root system, making them very difficult to uproot. • it would obey you: This is not a call to use faith for arbitrary miracles. God has unlimited power, and those who trust in him will see him use it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:6

## Luke 17:7–10 (id: 20656)

In Greco\-Roman culture, servants were expected to serve their masters faithfully. Faith ([17:5–6](https://ref.ly/Luke17:5-Luke17:6)) includes obeying Christ and submitting to his commands.

This healing reveals the compassion and power of Jesus. In Luke, the blessings of salvation are joyfully received by many outside Israel.

## Luke 17:12 (id: 20639)

*They stood at a distance*: See study note on 5:12. People with leprosy had to stay away from others and shout, “Unclean, unclean!” ([Leviticus 13:45–46](https://ref.ly/Lev13:45-Lev13:46)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:12
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 5:12 (ID: `20978@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 17:14 (id: 20640)

* *“Go, show yourselves to the priests.”*: [Leviticus 14:2–32](https://ref.ly/Lev14:2-Lev14:32) guides priests on how to determine what is leprosy and declare a leper ceremonially clean (see study notes on Luke 5:12, 14).
* *as they were in their way*: To leave without yet being healed required faith, which Jesus was testing (compare [2 Kings 5:9–14](https://ref.ly/2Kgs5:9-2Kgs5:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:14

The one man who returned to thank Jesus was a Samaritan*,* a hated foreigner in the eyes of most Jews (see study note on 10:33).

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Samaritans (ID: `group:Samaritan`)

## Luke 17:19 (id: 20642)

*your faith has made you well!* (or *your faith has saved you*): The Greek verb can refer to either physical or spiritual healing. The physical healings of Jesus show the salvation that his kingdom brings.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Luke 17:20–21 (id: 20644)

When will the Kingdom of God come? The common understanding among Jews was that the Messiah would establish God’s Kingdom in Jerusalem; he would defeat Israel’s enemies and bring in a period of peace, prosperity, justice, and righteousness (see [3:15](https://ref.ly/Luke3:15); cp. [Acts 1:6](https://ref.ly/Acts1:6)). The Pharisees were apparently challenging Jesus’ claim to be the Messiah because he was not defeating the Romans or establishing his Kingdom physically on earth. Jesus did not reject this future manifestation of the Kingdom (see [Luke 21:27](https://ref.ly/Luke21:27); [Matt 24:30–31](https://ref.ly/Matt24:30-Matt24:31)) but pointed out that the Kingdom of God was being revealed to them through his ministry, though they were missing it. • The Kingdom of God can’t be detected by visible signs: Jewish apocalyptic literature of Jesus’ day looked for visible signs in the heavens to signal the coming of the Messiah’s kingdom. Jesus elsewhere affirmed that such signs would appear (see [Luke 17:24](https://ref.ly/Luke17:24); [21:25](https://ref.ly/Luke21:25); [Acts 2:19–20](https://ref.ly/Acts2:19-Acts2:20)), but the Pharisees were missing the manifestation of the Kingdom in Jesus’ immediate ministry (cp. [Luke 7:22](https://ref.ly/Luke7:22)). • the Kingdom of God is already among you (or *is within you,* or *is in your grasp*): The Kingdom of God was already being revealed through Jesus’ words and actions. It is unlikely that Jesus would say, “The Kingdom of God is within you,” to those who were rejecting his message.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Observation (ID: `keyterm:Observation`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

This is the first of two discourses in Luke’s Gospel on the coming of the Kingdom and the return of the Son of Man (see also ch [21](https://ref.ly/Luke21:1-Luke21:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:20-37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Observation (ID: `keyterm:Observation`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Make Alive (ID: `keyterm:Alive`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Boat (ID: `realia:Boat`); Vulture (ID: `fauna:Vulture`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Luke 17:22 (id: 20646)

you will long to see the day when the Son of Man returns (or *you will long for even one day with the Son of Man*): The Greek says simply, *you will long to see the days of the Son of Man,* which may refer to his time on earth with his disciples or to the time of his second coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 17:23–24 (id: 20647)

People will tell you, ‘Look, there is the Son of Man’: False Messiahs have arisen throughout history, but expectations for the Messiah were particularly high in the first century, as a variety of figures claimed to be God’s agents of deliverance (see [Acts 5:36–37](https://ref.ly/Acts5:36-Acts5:37); [21:38](https://ref.ly/Acts21:38)). Jesus warned his disciples not to follow these imposters. • For as the lightning flashes: Lightning lights up the whole sky and is visible to everyone in the area. Similarly, when Jesus suddenly returns, it will be evident to everyone.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:23-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 17:25 (id: 20648)

*suffer many things*:Jesus would soon die by crucifixion (see study note on 9:22). Crucifixion was a form of execution used by the Romans. The victim was nailed or tied to a wooden cross and left to die.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:25
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 9:22 (ID: `21106@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`)

## Luke 17:26–29 (id: 20649)

in Noah’s day . . . in the days of Lot: See [Gen 6–9](https://ref.ly/Gen6:1-Gen9:29) for Noah and [Gen 18:16–19:29](https://ref.ly/Gen18:16-Gen19:29) for Lot; both are stories of God’s cataclysmic judgment against human wickedness.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:26-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Boat (ID: `realia:Boat`)

## Luke 17:32–33 (id: 20651)

Lot’s wife turned into a pillar of salt when she looked longingly back at Sodom ([Gen 19:26](https://ref.ly/Gen19:26)). She exemplifies those who cling to . . . life.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:32-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Make Alive (ID: `keyterm:Alive`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 17:34–35 (id: 20652)

one will be taken, the other left: Some consider this separation to refer to the Rapture of the church, but the parallels drawn to God’s judgment in the Flood and against Sodom ([17:26–29](https://ref.ly/Luke17:26-Luke17:29)), as well as the reference to vultures and a dead body ([17:37](https://ref.ly/Luke17:37)), suggest that it occurs at the final judgment (see [Mal 3:18](https://ref.ly/Mal3:18); [Matt 25:32](https://ref.ly/Matt25:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:34-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Vulture (ID: `fauna:Vulture`)

## Luke 18:1–8 (id: 20657)

The parable of the persistent widow follows naturally from the teaching of Jesus about coming troubles ([17:20–37](https://ref.ly/Luke17:20-Luke17:37)). Believers can face trials and persevere through persistent prayer.

## Luke 18:2 (id: 20673)

a judge . . . who neither feared God nor cared about people: The two most important attributes for a good judge are regard for justice (fear of God), and compassion for people (see [2 Chr 19:6–7](https://ref.ly/2Chr19:6-2Chr19:7)). This judge had neither.

## Luke 18:3 (id: 20682)

A widow: God is concerned for widows, orphans, and foreigners—the most vulnerable members of ancient society—and has promised judgment against those who oppress them ([Exod 22:22](https://ref.ly/Exod22:22); [Deut 10:18](https://ref.ly/Deut10:18); [24:17](https://ref.ly/Deut24:17); [27:19](https://ref.ly/Deut27:19); [Ps 68:5](https://ref.ly/Ps68:5); [Isa 1:23](https://ref.ly/Isa1:23); [10:2](https://ref.ly/Isa10:2); [Jer 22:3](https://ref.ly/Jer22:3); [Ezek 22:7](https://ref.ly/Ezek22:7); [Amos 5:10–13](https://ref.ly/Amos5:10-Amos5:13); [Zech 7:10](https://ref.ly/Zech7:10); [Mal 3:5](https://ref.ly/Mal3:5)). • Give me justice: The woman was in the right in this dispute—she was not asking for special favors.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`)

## Luke 18:5 (id: 20683)

*Will not God bring about justice*: If persistence resulted in justice from this unjust judge, how much more will God, who loves both people and justice, answer our prayers.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`)

when the Son of Man returns: This is an allusion to [Dan 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14). • how many will he find on the earth who have faith? ## Luke 18:12 (id: 20659)

I fast twice a week, and I give you a tenth of my income: Fasting and tithing were signs of piety in Judaism. The law required fasting only once a year ([Lev 16:29–31](https://ref.ly/Lev16:29-Lev16:31)), but pious Jews in Jesus’ day fasted twice a week (see also [Luke 5:33](https://ref.ly/Luke5:33); [11:41–42](https://ref.ly/Luke11:41-Luke11:42)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Week (ID: `keyterm:Week`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 18:14 (id: 20661)

Jesus’ conclusion that only the tax collector went home justified before God would have shocked Jesus’ audience, who regarded Pharisees as righteous and tax collectors as wicked. • those who exalt themselves will be humbled: See [1:52–53](https://ref.ly/Luke1:52-Luke1:53); [6:21](https://ref.ly/Luke6:21), [25](https://ref.ly/Luke6:25); [10:15](https://ref.ly/Luke10:15); [14:11](https://ref.ly/Luke14:11); [16:19–31](https://ref.ly/Luke16:19-Luke16:31).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 18:15–17 (id: 20662)

This incident illustrates [18:14](https://ref.ly/Luke18:14). • they scolded the parents: In this cultural context, children had no social status (see study note on 9:48), so the disciples viewed them as an interruption to Jesus’ important ministry.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:15-17
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 9:48 (ID: `21128@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 18:17 (id: 20663)

*receive the kingdom of God like a little child*: Entrance into the kingdom of God requires childlike faith and dependence on God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`)

## Luke 18:18 (id: 20665)

* The man was probably a religious leader, not a political ruler.
* *what must I do to inherit eternal life?*: This question was commonly discussed by the rabbis in the time of Jesus (see [10:27](https://ref.ly/Luke10:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 18:18–30 (id: 20664)

The story of the rich religious leader warns against trusting in riches rather than pledging complete allegiance to God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:18-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`); Rich Young Ruler (ID: `person:RichYoungRuler`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Needle (ID: `realia:Needle`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`)

## Luke 18:19 (id: 20666)

*Why do you call Me good?*: The man called Jesus *good* as a compliment, but only God is truly good. Jesus challenged the man's understanding of goodness. True goodness requires moral perfection.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 18:20 (id: 20667)

*You know the commandments*: Jesus refers to the fifth through ninth of the Ten Commandments ([Exodus 20:12–16](https://ref.ly/Exod20:12-Exod20:16); [Deuteronomy 5:16–20](https://ref.ly/Deut5:16-Deut5:20)). These commandments are about relationships between human beings.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`)

## Luke 18:21 (id: 20668)

*All these I have kept*: The man’s claim to have kept all the commandments shows that he misunderstood the nature of true goodness.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rich Young Ruler (ID: `person:RichYoungRuler`)

## Luke 18:22 (id: 20669)

The man's love for his wealth showed he did not fully obey God. He did not love God or others as God requires ([10:25–28](https://ref.ly/Luke10:25-Luke10:28)). Loving wealth is a form of worshiping false gods (idolatry) ([Ephesians 5:5](https://ref.ly/Eph5:5); [Colossians 3:5](https://ref.ly/Col3:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 18:24–25 (id: 20670)

How hard it is for the rich to enter the Kingdom of God! Though Scripture sometimes speaks of riches as a blessing from God ([2 Chr 1:11–12](https://ref.ly/2Chr1:11-2Chr1:12); [Pss 112:3](https://ref.ly/Ps112:3); [128:2](https://ref.ly/Ps128:2); [Prov 8:18](https://ref.ly/Prov8:18); [Isa 61:6](https://ref.ly/Isa61:6)), many passages warn against the danger of trusting riches instead of God ([Ps 62:10](https://ref.ly/Ps62:10); [Prov 11:28](https://ref.ly/Prov11:28); [Jer 9:23–24](https://ref.ly/Jer9:23-Jer9:24); [49:4–5](https://ref.ly/Jer49:4-Jer49:5)). • the eye of a needle: See study note on Mark 10:25. This is not merely difficult, but impossible. No one can be saved while trusting in riches. Salvation comes only by dependence on God ([Luke 18:27](https://ref.ly/Luke18:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:24-25
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 10:25 (ID: `21237@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Needle (ID: `realia:Needle`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`)

## Luke 18:27 (id: 20671)

The point of this whole event is that salvation is impossible by human effort, but possible by the grace of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 18:28–30 (id: 20672)

*we have left all we had to follow You*: In contrast to the rich ruler, Peter and the other disciples had given up everything to follow Jesus. Jesus affirmed that they would receive back far more than they had given up.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:28-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 18:30 (id: 20674)

*receive many times more in this age*: Their reward would not necessarily be physical, but they would enjoy the blessings that come from spiritual wholeness and a right relationship with God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 18:31 (id: 20676)

The death of Jesus fulfilled all the predictions of the Old Testament prophets. It was God's plan ([Luke 24:25–26](https://ref.ly/Luke24:25-Luke24:26), [46](https://ref.ly/Luke24:46); [Acts 2:23](https://ref.ly/Acts2:23); [3:18](https://ref.ly/Acts3:18); [4:28](https://ref.ly/Acts4:28)). 

The main prophecy about the suffering Messiah is in [Isaiah 52:13–53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:13-Isa53:12). Luke also refers to several Old Testament prophecies, including: 

* [Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12) in [Acts 4:25–26](https://ref.ly/Acts4:25-Acts4:26),
* [Psalm 16](https://ref.ly/Ps16:1-Ps16:11) in [Acts 2:25–28](https://ref.ly/Acts2:25-Acts2:28),
* [Psalm 118:22](https://ref.ly/Ps118:22) in [Luke 20:17](https://ref.ly/Luke20:17), and
* [Isaiah 50:4–9](https://ref.ly/Isa50:4-Isa50:9) in [Luke 18:32–33](https://ref.ly/Luke18:32-Luke18:33).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 18:31–34 (id: 20675)

This was the last time Jesus predicted his suffering and death before he entered Jerusalem for his final week of ministry (see also [9:22](https://ref.ly/Luke9:22), [44–45](https://ref.ly/Luke9:44-Luke9:45); [17:25](https://ref.ly/Luke17:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:31-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`)

## Luke 18:32–33 (id: 20677)

*will be mocked …* *will flog Him*: See [Isaiah 50:6](https://ref.ly/Isa50:6).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:32-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`)

## Luke 18:34 (id: 20678)

*The disciples did not understand any of these things*: The disciples did not understand that the death of Jesus would bring salvation until after he rose from the dead (see [9:45](https://ref.ly/Luke9:45); [24:13–34](https://ref.ly/Luke24:13-Luke24:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`)

## Luke 18:35 (id: 20680)

Jericho was located in an oasis in the Judean wilderness, 30 kilometers (18 miles) northeast of Jerusalem. At 250 meters (820 feet) below sea level, it is the world's lowest city and one of the oldest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`)

## Luke 18:35–43 (id: 20679)

This account again demonstrates Jesus’ care for the poor and marginalized in Israel. It reminds the reader that Jesus is the Son of David, the Messiah ([18:38](https://ref.ly/Luke18:38)), shortly before he enters Jerusalem as king ([19:28–44](https://ref.ly/Luke19:28-Luke19:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:35-43
