## Luke 18:38 (id: 20681)

Son of David is a title for the Messiah, a descendant of David who would reign on David’s throne forever (see [1:32–33](https://ref.ly/Luke1:32-Luke1:33); [2 Sam 7:11–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:11-2Sam7:16); [Isa 9:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa9:6-Isa9:7); [11:1–5](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:5); [Jer 23:5–6](https://ref.ly/Jer23:5-Jer23:6); [33:15–16](https://ref.ly/Jer33:15-Jer33:16); [Ezek 37:24–25](https://ref.ly/Ezek37:24-Ezek37:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); David (ID: `person:David`); Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`)

## Luke 19:1–10 (id: 20687)

The story of Zacchaeus is a fitting climax to Jesus’ ministry to the outcasts of Israel on his journey to Jerusalem ([9:51–19:44](https://ref.ly/Luke9:51-Luke19:44)). The final verse of the episode ([19:10](https://ref.ly/Luke19:10)) is often viewed as the theme verse of Luke’s Gospel.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Zacchaeus (ID: `person:Zacchaeus`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`); Sycomore Fig (ID: `flora:SycomoreFig`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 19:2 (id: 20698)

The chief tax collector for a particular region leased from the Romans the right to collect taxes, and then oversaw subordinate tax collectors from whom he collected a commission. This situation allowed Zacchaeus to get very rich, and chief tax collectors were hated even more than their subordinates (see study note on 3:12).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:2
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:12 (ID: `20927@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zacchaeus (ID: `person:Zacchaeus`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 19:4 (id: 20709)

climbed a sycamore\-fig tree: This act was very undignified for a man of Zacchaeus’ power and wealth; it shows his genuine spiritual interest in Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sycomore Fig (ID: `flora:SycomoreFig`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`)

## Luke 19:5 (id: 20710)

Zacchaeus! . . . I must be a guest: Jesus knew Zacchaeus’ name and invited himself to Zacchaeus’ home, emphasizing his divine authority and that this appointment was made by God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zacchaeus (ID: `person:Zacchaeus`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 19:7 (id: 20711)

*The guest of a sinful man*: People also criticized Jesus for spending time with sinners, as they did when he was with Levi ([5:27–32](https://ref.ly/Luke5:27-Luke5:32); see study note on 5:30).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:7
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 5:30 (ID: `20990@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 19:8 (id: 20712)

I will give them back four times: Normal restitution for a wrong committed was to add twenty percent to the value of the goods lost ([Lev 5:16](https://ref.ly/Lev5:16); [Num 5:7](https://ref.ly/Num5:7)), though the penalty for theft of an animal was four or five times its value ([Exod 22:1](https://ref.ly/Exod22:1)). Zacchaeus apparently regarded his financial gains as theft and promised the required restitution.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zacchaeus (ID: `person:Zacchaeus`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 19:9 (id: 20713)

this man has shown himself to be a true son of Abraham: Zacchaeus was a child of Abraham by descent (a Jew); now he demonstrated faith like Abraham’s (see [Rom 4:11–12](https://ref.ly/Rom4:11-Rom4:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Luke 19:10 (id: 20688)

to seek and save those who are lost: The Lord is a shepherd who seeks lost sheep (see [Ps 23:1](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1); [Isa 53:6](https://ref.ly/Isa53:6); [Jer 13:17](https://ref.ly/Jer13:17); [Ezek 34](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:1-Ezek34:31); [Zech 10:3](https://ref.ly/Zech10:3); [13:7](https://ref.ly/Zech13:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Luke 19:11–27 (id: 20689)

This parable has two main purposes: (1\) to teach stewardship, the need to manage gifts and resources well while the king (Jesus) is away (cp. [Matt 25:14–30](https://ref.ly/Matt25:14-Matt25:30)); and (2\) to correct the impression that the Kingdom of God would begin right away upon Jesus’ entrance into Jerusalem. Most Jews of Jesus’ day expected that when the Messiah came, God’s Kingdom would be established physically on earth, with Jerusalem at its center (see [Isa 2:2–4](https://ref.ly/Isa2:2-Isa2:4); [35:1–10](https://ref.ly/Isa35:1-Isa35:10); [65:17–25](https://ref.ly/Isa65:17-Isa65:25); [Jer 30–31](https://ref.ly/Jer30:1-Jer31:40); [Ezek 37](https://ref.ly/Ezek37:1-Ezek37:28), [40–48](https://ref.ly/Ezek40:1-Ezek48:35); [Mic 4:1–5](https://ref.ly/Mic4:1-Mic4:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:11-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mina (ID: `realia:Mina`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Band of Cloth (ID: `realia:BandOfCloth`)

## Luke 19:12 (id: 20690)

A nobleman was called away to a distant empire to be crowned king: The account fits the situation of Judea as a client kingdom of the Roman Empire. After the death of a king, claimants to the throne would go to Rome to try to gain support. Like the nobleman, Jesus would first depart (to heaven) to receive his royal authority; he will later return to rule God’s people and judge those who have opposed him (see [19:14](https://ref.ly/Luke19:14), [27](https://ref.ly/Luke19:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 19:14 (id: 20691)

his people hated him and sent a delegation: This happened to Archelaus, the son of Herod the Great, who violently put down protests after the death of his father. In response, the Jews sent a delegation to plead against his kingship before the Roman emperor.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:14


## Luke 19:17 (id: 20692)

you will be governor of ten cities as your reward: Because of the servant’s faithfulness, the king gave him a major position in his kingdom. Similarly, those who are faithful to Jesus in this life will receive greater responsibility here and great rewards in heaven.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`)

## Luke 19:23 (id: 20693)

deposit my money in the bank: Literally *put the money on the table*, which means to give it to moneylenders who would loan it out at interest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:23


## Luke 19:26 (id: 20694)

*will be given more*: Those who are trustworthy with a small amount will receive more (compare [8:18](https://ref.ly/Luke8:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:26


## Luke 19:27 (id: 20695)

as for these enemies of mine: Those who reject Jesus’ reign as king will suffer his judgment. This part of the parable was directed against the religious leaders. • bring them in and execute them: A new king would often execute his opponents (see [1 Kgs 2:13–46](https://ref.ly/1Kgs2:13-1Kgs2:46); cp. [Ps 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:27


## Luke 19:28–40 (id: 20696)

Jesus entered Jerusalem in triumph. This fulfilled [Zechariah 9:9–10](https://ref.ly/Zech9:9-Zech9:10) and symbolically announced that he was the Messiah, the King of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:28-40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Height (ID: `keyterm:Height`); Mount of Olives (ID: `place:OlivesMount`); Bethphage (ID: `place:Bethphage`); Bethany (ID: `place:Bethany`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Luke 19:29 (id: 20697)

Bethany was on the eastern slope of the Mount of Olives, two miles east of Jerusalem; it was the home of Lazarus and his sisters, Mary and Martha ([John 11:1](https://ref.ly/John11:1), [18](https://ref.ly/John11:18); [12:1](https://ref.ly/John12:1); see [Luke 10:38](https://ref.ly/Luke10:38)). The location of Bethphage is uncertain, but it was probably near Bethany on the same road from Jericho. • The Mount of Olives overlooks Jerusalem from the east and has significance at the return of Christ (see [Acts 1:11](https://ref.ly/Acts1:11); [Zech 14:4](https://ref.ly/Zech14:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethany (ID: `place:Bethany`); Mount of Olives (ID: `place:OlivesMount`); Bethphage (ID: `place:Bethphage`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`)

## Luke 19:30 (id: 20699)

you will see a young donkey: It is unclear whether Jesus had arranged for the donkey ahead of time, or whether he used divine insight. Either way, Jesus’ entrance into Jerusalem was a symbolic action. He rode a humble *donkey* rather than a war horse to confirm that he was fulfilling the role of the Messiah by bringing reconciliation and peace ([Zech 9:9–10](https://ref.ly/Zech9:9-Zech9:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

## Luke 19:31 (id: 20700)

The Lord needs it: Subjects of a king were expected to make resources available for his use (see [1 Sam 8:16](https://ref.ly/1Sam8:16)). The donkey’s colt had never been ridden, so it was pure and suitable for a king.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 19:36 (id: 20701)

The crowds *spread their cloaks* to show respect and honor to this royal figure (see [2 Kings 9:13](https://ref.ly/2Kgs9:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 19:38 (id: 20702)

Blessings on the King . . . glory in highest heaven! See [Pss 118:26](https://ref.ly/Ps118:26); [148:1](https://ref.ly/Ps148:1). [Psalm 118](https://ref.ly/Ps118:1-Ps118:29) was one of the psalms sung by pilgrims traveling to Jerusalem for one of the festivals ([Pss 113–118](https://ref.ly/Ps113:1-Ps118:29)). • Jesus brought peace in heaven—reconciliation between God and human beings—but peace on earth awaits the future (cp. [Luke 2:14](https://ref.ly/Luke2:14); see [12:51–53](https://ref.ly/Luke12:51-Luke12:53); [19:41–44](https://ref.ly/Luke19:41-Luke19:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Height (ID: `keyterm:Height`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Luke 19:40 (id: 20703)

Compare [Isa 55:12](https://ref.ly/Isa55:12), in which nature is said to rejoice at the coming of God’s salvation, and [Hab 2:11](https://ref.ly/Hab2:11), in which the stones of the walls cry out against Babylon.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:40


## Luke 19:42 (id: 20704)

*now it is hidden from your eyes*: Israel’s rejection of Jesus had serious consequences. In this context, the decision was final and could not be reversed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`)

## Luke 19:43–44 (id: 20705)

Jesus foretold the siege and destruction of Jerusalem by the Romans in AD 70\. The Jewish historian Josephus describes the horrific suffering of the inhabitants of Jerusalem during the Roman siege (*War* 5\.1\.1–7\.1\.1\). • will not leave a single stone in place: This is an image of total devastation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:43-44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Siege-Works (ID: `realia:Siege-works`)

## Luke 19:45 (id: 20707)

Pilgrims coming to worship at the Temple would purchase animals for sacrifices in the Temple courts. Jesus also drove out the money changers who provided the correct currency for the Temple tax ([Matt 21:12](https://ref.ly/Matt21:12); [Mark 11:15](https://ref.ly/Mark11:15); see [Exod 30:13–16](https://ref.ly/Exod30:13-Exod30:16); [Neh 10:32–33](https://ref.ly/Neh10:32-Neh10:33)). While these were necessary services, Jesus objected to the exploitation of the Temple for illicit commercial gain that distracted from its purpose as a place of worship. The Temple was meant to reflect God’s glory for all nations, but it had become corrupt.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 19:45–48 (id: 20706)

Having just entered Jerusalem as the Messiah, Jesus performed a messianic action (cp. [Mal 3:1](https://ref.ly/Mal3:1)) by driving money changers and merchants selling animals for sacrifices out of the Temple. This cleansing was to restore true worship to the Temple. It was also symbolic of the judgment that Jesus had just pronounced against Israel ([19:41–44](https://ref.ly/Luke19:41-Luke19:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:45-48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 19:47 (id: 20708)

The clearing of the Temple catalyzed the opposition and precipitated Jesus’ crucifixion (but see study note on Mark 11:12–25). • Jesus’ opposition in Galilee came mainly from the Pharisees and teachers of religious law; in Jerusalem, the opposition also included the leading priests*,* who controlled the Temple. Jesus’ actions against the Temple trade threatened their authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:47
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 11:12-25 (ID: `21264@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 20:1 (id: 20758)

the leading priests, the teachers of religious law, and the elders: The most influential Jewish leaders in Jerusalem converged against Jesus, who threatened their authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 20:1–47 (id: 20751)

In this series of controversies between Jesus and the religious leaders of Jerusalem, they repeatedly questioned and challenged him, and he repeatedly outmatched them with his spiritual wisdom, insight, and authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:1-47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); David (ID: `person:David`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Empty (ID: `keyterm:Empty`); John (ID: `person:John`); Friendship (ID: `keyterm:Friendship`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); A Priest (ID: `person:GenericPriest`); Hypocrisy (ID: `keyterm:Hypocrisy`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Tribute Tax (ID: `keyterm:TributeTax`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Sadducees (ID: `group:Sadducee`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Cornerstone (ID: `realia:Cornerstone`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Footstool (ID: `realia:Footstool`)

## Luke 20:2 (id: 20767)

By what authority are you doing all these things? Since these groups were the recognized Jewish authorities in Jerusalem, they wanted to know how Jesus could claim authority to enter Jerusalem as a king and drive the merchants from the Temple.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 20:4–6 (id: 20773)

The question Jesus asked trapped the religious leaders (see [20:5–6](https://ref.ly/Luke20:5-Luke20:6)) because either answer would accuse them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); John (ID: `person:John`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 20:6 (id: 20781)

the people will stone us: Stoning was the penalty for blasphemy ([Lev 24:14–15](https://ref.ly/Lev24:14-Lev24:15)), idolatry ([Lev 20:2](https://ref.ly/Lev20:2); [Deut 13:10](https://ref.ly/Deut13:10)), and other sins of defiance against God. The people might have regarded rejection of an authentic prophet as blasphemy deserving of stoning.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 20:7–8 (id: 20782)

The Jewish authorities refused to answer Jesus’ question, so Jesus had no obligation to answer their question. Both their question and their refusal were guided by self\-interest rather than integrity.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 20:9 (id: 20784)

leased it to tenant farmers: This was a common practice in first\-century Palestine, especially in Galilee, where peasants would lease the right to raise crops from wealthy landowners.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 20:9–19 (id: 20783)

This story adapts Isaiah’s Song of the Fruitful Vineyard ([Isa 5:1–7](https://ref.ly/Isa5:1-Isa5:7)) to allegorize Jesus’ rejection by Israel’s leaders. In Isaiah’s allegory, the vineyard owner is God and Israel is the unfruitful vineyard that will be judged. Jesus added new characters—the tenant farmers were Israel’s unrighteous leaders, the servants were the Old Testament prophets (repeatedly rejected by Israel), and the son was Jesus. God would judge Israel’s leaders for rejecting and murdering his son.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:9-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Empty (ID: `keyterm:Empty`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Cornerstone (ID: `realia:Cornerstone`)

## Luke 20:10 (id: 20752)

*the tenants beat the servan**t*: God’s prophets were often mistreated and opposed by the people of Israel ([1 Kings 19:10](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:10), [14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:14); [2 Chronicles 24:21](https://ref.ly/2Chr24:21); [36:16](https://ref.ly/2Chr36:16); [Nehemiah 9:26](https://ref.ly/Neh9:26); [Jeremiah 2:30](https://ref.ly/Jer2:30); [26:20–24](https://ref.ly/Jer26:20-Jer26:24); [37:15](https://ref.ly/Jer37:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Empty (ID: `keyterm:Empty`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 20:13 (id: 20753)

*my beloved son*: The Messiah was predicted to have a unique father\-son relationship with God (see [2 Samuel 7:14](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:14); [Psalms 2:7](https://ref.ly/Ps2:7); [89:26–29](https://ref.ly/Ps89:26-Ps89:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 20:14 (id: 20754)

*Let us kill him, and the inheritance will be ours*: They believed that killing the heir would give them power over the estate. They foolishly failed to realize that the owner was still alive and would demand justice. Similarly, the religious leaders thought that they could keep their authority over the people of Israel by killing Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:14


## Luke 20:17 (id: 20755)

The stone . . . cornerstone: Jesus cited [Ps 118:22](https://ref.ly/Ps118:22) to predict his rejection and restoration. The cornerstone was the key part of a building, used to support and align adjoining walls. Jesus was rejected by the builders (the religious leaders), but he would become the foundation of a new building (the church).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Cornerstone (ID: `realia:Cornerstone`)

## Luke 20:18 (id: 20756)

Jesus expanded the stone metaphor ([20:17](https://ref.ly/Luke20:17)) with allusions to [Isa 8:14–15](https://ref.ly/Isa8:14-Isa8:15) and [Dan 2:34](https://ref.ly/Dan2:34), [44–45](https://ref.ly/Dan2:44-Dan2:45). Although Israel rejected him, Jesus was the foundation for the new people of God, and he inaugurated a Kingdom that would last forever.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:18


## Luke 20:19 (id: 20757)

*realized that Jesus had spoken this parable against them*: The story provoked the religious leaders to act it out by arresting and killing Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`)

## Luke 20:20–26 (id: 20759)

The religious leaders asked this question to trap Jesus, but he answered in a way that avoided their trap.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:20-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Hypocrisy (ID: `keyterm:Hypocrisy`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Tribute Tax (ID: `keyterm:TributeTax`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Luke 20:21 (id: 20760)

*we know that You speak and teach correctly*: These dishonest words of praise were meant to trick Jesus (see [Psalms 5:9](https://ref.ly/Ps5:9); [12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Ps12:2-Ps12:3); [78:36](https://ref.ly/Ps78:36); [Proverbs 26:28](https://ref.ly/Prov26:28); [28:23](https://ref.ly/Prov28:23); [29:5](https://ref.ly/Prov29:5); [Ezekiel 12:24](https://ref.ly/Ezek12:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`)

## Luke 20:22 (id: 20761)

*Is it lawful for us to pay taxes to Caesar or not?*: If Jesus said yes, he would make the people angry, because they hated the burden of Roman taxation. If he said no, he would be accused of rebellion against the Roman authorities.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Tribute Tax (ID: `keyterm:TributeTax`); Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`)

## Luke 20:24 (id: 20762)

*a denarius*: This Roman coin had the image and inscription of the emperor Tiberius Caesar (see [3:1](https://ref.ly/Luke3:1)) stamped on it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`); Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Luke 20:25 (id: 20763)

*Give to Caesar what is Caesar’s*: Jesus gave a clever answer. There are two ways to understand this statement. On the surface, he said that the coin belonged to Caesar and should be given to him. But his Jewish listeners also understood that, in the end, nothing truly belongs to Caesar, because everything belongs to God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`)

## Luke 20:27 (id: 20764)

*the Sadducees, who say there is no resurrection*: See "The Sadducees" Profile.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:27
* **Associated Articles:** The Sadducees (ID: `500538@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Sadducees (ID: `group:Sadducee`)

## Luke 20:28 (id: 20765)

*Moses wrote for us*: A dead man’s brother would marry the widow to produce children who would maintain the dead man’s name and property ([Deuteronomy 25:5–6](https://ref.ly/Deut25:5-Deut25:6); see also [Genesis 38:8–10](https://ref.ly/Gen38:8-Gen38:10); [Ruth 4:1–12](https://ref.ly/Ruth4:1-Ruth4:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Luke 20:29–33 (id: 20766)

Well, suppose there were seven brothers: A similar story of a woman married to seven husbands appears in the Old Testament Apocrypha (*Tobit* 3:7–17; 6:10–8:18\).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:29-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 20:33 (id: 20768)

*in the resurrection, whose wife will she be?*: The Sadducees told this made\-up story to show that the idea of resurrection (rising from the dead) did not make sense. They wanted people to think that belief in resurrection was foolish.

They likely used this same argument many times. It was a common attack that the Sadducees used when they debated the Pharisees.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 20:35 (id: 20769)

*will neither marry nor be given in marriage*: Jesus taught that marriage belongs to this present life on earth. It is not part of life after the resurrection (when the dead rise to life again).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 20:36 (id: 20770)

In this respect they will be like angels: Angels are not married, and they are immortal. The Bible never teaches that people become angels (cp.[Heb 1:5–2:18](https://ref.ly/Heb1:5-Heb2:18)), but that we will be like them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 20:37 (id: 20771)

Having refuted the Sadducees’ argument about marriage, Jesus turned to the larger question concerning the resurrection. • even Moses proved this: Jesus used the books of Moses (Genesis—Deuteronomy)—the only Scripture the Sadducees recognized as authoritative—to prove the resurrection. • the God of Abraham . . . Jacob: God identified himself as the God of Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob long after these patriarchs had died. Since he was still their God, they must have had a continuing existence after death.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`)

## Luke 20:39 (id: 20772)

Well said, Teacher! Most teachers of religious law were Pharisees, so they agreed with Jesus on the resurrection and were pleased that he refuted the Sadducees.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); A Priest (ID: `person:GenericPriest`)

## Luke 20:40 (id: 20774)

The wise answers of Jesus confused his opponents. They could not defeat him in argument.

They had tried to trap him with difficult questions. Instead, his replies exposed their motives and showed his wisdom.

As a result, their plans to trap him failed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:40


## Luke 20:41 (id: 20776)

The Old Testament predicted that the Messiah would be a descendant of King David (see study note on 18:38).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:41
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 18:38 (ID: `20681@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); David (ID: `person:David`)

## Luke 20:41–44 (id: 20775)

After Jesus silenced his opponents, he began to challenge the religious leaders and their understanding of the Messiah.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:41-44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Footstool (ID: `realia:Footstool`)

## Luke 20:42–43 (id: 20777)

Jesus quotes [Psalm 110:1\.](https://ref.ly/Ps110:1) By quoting this verse, Jesus shows that the Messiah (God's chosen leader) is more than a descendant of David. The Messiah is also David’s Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:42-43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); David (ID: `person:David`); Footstool (ID: `realia:Footstool`)

## Luke 20:44 (id: 20778)

Jesus asked why David addressed his son (i.e., his descendant) as his Lord. The implied answer was that the Messiah was more than just a human king descended from David. He is Lord of all (see [2:11](https://ref.ly/Luke2:11); [Acts 10:36](https://ref.ly/Acts10:36)). [Psalm 110:1](https://ref.ly/Ps110:1) became an important text for the apostles, confirming Jesus’ identity as the Son of God (see [Mark 14:62](https://ref.ly/Mark14:62); [Acts 2:34](https://ref.ly/Acts2:34); [7:56](https://ref.ly/Acts7:56); [Rom 8:34](https://ref.ly/Rom8:34); [1 Cor 15:25](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:25); [Eph 1:20](https://ref.ly/Eph1:20); [Col 3:1](https://ref.ly/Col3:1); [Heb 1:3](https://ref.ly/Heb1:3), [13](https://ref.ly/Heb1:13); [8:1](https://ref.ly/Heb8:1); [10:12–13](https://ref.ly/Heb10:12-Heb10:13); [1 Pet 3:22](https://ref.ly/1Pet3:22); [Rev 3:21](https://ref.ly/Rev3:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`)

## Luke 20:46–47 (id: 20779)

Jesus continued on the offensive, warning against the hypocrisy and exploitation of the teachers of religious law. • respectful greetings . . . seats of honor: See study note on 11:43. • head table at banquets: See study notes on 5:30; 14:7.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:46-47
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 11:43 (ID: `20509@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 14:7 (ID: `20596@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 5:30 (ID: `20990@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Friendship (ID: `keyterm:Friendship`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 20:47 (id: 20780)

Yet they shamelessly cheat widows: This might mean exploiting widows’ property over which they were appointed guardians; it could also mean coercing widows to give money beyond their means. Widows were the most vulnerable members of society, and God had special concern for them. He would judge those who exploited them ([Exod 22:22](https://ref.ly/Exod22:22); [Deut 10:18](https://ref.ly/Deut10:18); [27:19](https://ref.ly/Deut27:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 21:1 (id: 20792)

The Greek term for *treasury* likely refers to boxes with trumpet\-shaped openings for collecting offerings.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Treasury (ID: `realia:Treasury`)

## Luke 21:1–4 (id: 20785)

Jesus set the sacrificial offering of the poor widow in contrast to the greed and hypocrisy of the rich people*,* including the teachers of religious law ([20:45–47](https://ref.ly/Luke20:45-Luke20:47)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); True (ID: `keyterm:True`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Lepton (ID: `realia:Lepton`); Treasury (ID: `realia:Treasury`)

## Luke 21:2 (id: 20798)

*two small copper coins*: These coins (Greek *lepta*) were worth about 1/64 of a denarius. A denarius was the wage a laborer would receive for one day of work.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lepton (ID: `realia:Lepton`)

## Luke 21:4 (id: 20804)

The size of the gift matters less than the attitude of the person who gives it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Luke 21:5 (id: 20806)

majestic stonework: Herod the Great rebuilt and restored the Jerusalem Temple, producing one of the great structures of the ancient world. According to Josephus, the massive white stones reflected the sun with such brilliance that from a distance the Temple looked like snow\-covered mountains (*War* 5\.5\.6\). • The memorial decorations were probably funded by offerings given by worshipers to fulfill vows.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 21:5–38 (id: 20805)

When Jesus predicted the destruction of the Temple of Jerusalem ([21:6](https://ref.ly/Luke21:6)), his disciples questioned when it would take place and what signs would accompany it ([21:7](https://ref.ly/Luke21:7)). Jesus responded by describing the events surrounding the destruction of the Temple and the return of the Son of Man ([21:8–36](https://ref.ly/Luke21:8-Luke21:36)). This sermon is known as the Olivet Discourse because the Mount of Olives was the site of Jesus’ teaching (see [Matt 24:3](https://ref.ly/Matt24:3); [Mark 13:3](https://ref.ly/Mark13:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:5-38
