*not one stone will be left on another*: See study note on 19:43–44.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:6
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 19:43-44 (ID: `20705@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 21:8 (id: 20809)

*claiming, ‘I am He,’*: See study note on Mark 13:5–6.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:8
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 13:5-6 (ID: `21345@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 21:8–11 (id: 20808)

Jesus spoke about events that many people misunderstand as signs of the end of the age. These events include false messiahs (people who claim to be God’s chosen one), wars, earthquakes, famines (times when there is not enough food), and severe persecution (violent suffering because of faith).

However, Jesus made it clear that these events do not mean the end will happen right away. The end will not come immediately after these things.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:8-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Dreadful Event (ID: `keyterm:DreadfulEventOrSight`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 21:11 (id: 20786)

Cataclysmic events such as these are often associated with the judgment of God ([1 Sam 14:15](https://ref.ly/1Sam14:15); [Ps 18:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps18:7-Ps18:8); [Isa 2:19](https://ref.ly/Isa2:19), [21](https://ref.ly/Isa2:21); [5:25](https://ref.ly/Isa5:25); [13:13](https://ref.ly/Isa13:13); [24:18](https://ref.ly/Isa24:18); [29:5–6](https://ref.ly/Isa29:5-Isa29:6); [Jer 14:12](https://ref.ly/Jer14:12); [21:6–7](https://ref.ly/Jer21:6-Jer21:7); [Ezek 14:21](https://ref.ly/Ezek14:21); [Amos 1:1](https://ref.ly/Amos1:1)) and the end of the age ([Isa 24:18](https://ref.ly/Isa24:18); [29:5–6](https://ref.ly/Isa29:5-Isa29:6); [Ezek 38:19](https://ref.ly/Ezek38:19); [Joel 2:10](https://ref.ly/Joel2:10); [Hag 2:6](https://ref.ly/Hag2:6), [21](https://ref.ly/Hag2:21); [Zech 14:4](https://ref.ly/Zech14:4); cp. [Rev 6:12](https://ref.ly/Rev6:12); [8:5](https://ref.ly/Rev8:5); [11:13](https://ref.ly/Rev11:13), [19](https://ref.ly/Rev11:19); [16:18](https://ref.ly/Rev16:18)). • great miraculous signs from heaven: See study note on 21:25.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:11
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 21:25 (ID: `20797@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dreadful Event (ID: `keyterm:DreadfulEventOrSight`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 21:12 (id: 20788)

dragged into synagogues: Jesus and his early followers were Jewish, and the earliest conflicts were with fellow Jews who did not accept Jesus as the Messiah. Jesus’ followers were sometimes tried in the synagogue before a Jewish tribunal (cp. [2 Cor 11:24](https://ref.ly/2Cor11:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 21:12–19 (id: 20787)

a time of great persecution: Jesus described the persecution that his followers would experience (see also [Matt 10:17–22](https://ref.ly/Matt10:17-Matt10:22); [Mark 13:9](https://ref.ly/Mark13:9)). The Jewish literature of Jesus’ day similarly described a time of great tribulation prior to the arrival of God’s final salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:12-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 21:15 (id: 20789)

I will give you the right words: God often assured his prophets that he would give them the words to say ([Exod 4:12](https://ref.ly/Exod4:12), [15](https://ref.ly/Exod4:15); [Deut 18:18](https://ref.ly/Deut18:18); [Isa 50:4](https://ref.ly/Isa50:4); [51:16](https://ref.ly/Isa51:16); [Jer 1:9](https://ref.ly/Jer1:9)). Jesus’ disciples were to prepare themselves to be clear witnesses (see [Col 4:5–6](https://ref.ly/Col4:5-Col4:6); [2 Tim 2:21](https://ref.ly/2Tim2:21); [1 Pet 3:15](https://ref.ly/1Pet3:15)), knowing that the Spirit would help them to respond as powerfully as Jesus had done ([Luke 20:20–44](https://ref.ly/Luke20:20-Luke20:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:15


## Luke 21:16 (id: 20790)

Families in Jewish and Gentile communities have often been divided as some members follow Jesus and some reject him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:16


## Luke 21:18–19 (id: 20791)

But not a hair of your head will perish! This idiom signifies full protection ([1 Sam 14:45](https://ref.ly/1Sam14:45); [2 Sam 14:11](https://ref.ly/2Sam14:11); [1 Kgs 1:52](https://ref.ly/1Kgs1:52); [Dan 3:27](https://ref.ly/Dan3:27)). It refers here to the disciples’ souls*,* because some would be martyred for their faith ([Luke 21:16](https://ref.ly/Luke21:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:18-19


## Luke 21:20–24 (id: 20793)

Jesus was describing the siege and destruction of Jerusalem that would take place in AD 70\.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:20-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`)

## Luke 21:21 (id: 20794)

*those in the city get out*: Because of this prophecy, Christians in Judea fled to the town of Pella in Decapolis before the destruction of Jerusalem in AD 70 (Eusebius, *Church History* 3\.5\).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judea (ID: `place:Judea`)

## Luke 21:22 (id: 20795)

*these are the days of vengeance*: The destruction of Jerusalem was part of God’s judgment for Israel’s rejection of the Messiah.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`)

## Luke 21:24 (id: 20796)

*until the times of the Gentiles are fulfilled*: Daniel predicted that a series of Gentile empires (kingdoms ruled by non\-Jews) would come before God’s kingdom ([Daniel 2:44](https://ref.ly/Dan2:44); [7:27](https://ref.ly/Dan7:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`)

## Luke 21:25 (id: 20797)

The prophets predicted that *signs* in the sky would come before God's judgment at the end of time ([Isaiah 13:9–10](https://ref.ly/Isa13:9-Isa13:10); [34:4](https://ref.ly/Isa34:4); [Jeremiah 4:23](https://ref.ly/Jer4:23), [28](https://ref.ly/Jer4:28); [Ezekiel 32:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ezek32:7-Ezek32:8); [Joel 2:10](https://ref.ly/Joel2:10), [30–31](https://ref.ly/Joel2:30-Joel2:31); compare [Revelation 6:13–14](https://ref.ly/Rev6:13-Rev6:14); [8:10](https://ref.ly/Rev8:10); [9:1](https://ref.ly/Rev9:1); [12:1](https://ref.ly/Rev12:1),[3](https://ref.ly/Rev12:3); [15:1](https://ref.ly/Luke15:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`)

## Luke 21:31 (id: 20799)

All these things refer to the events described in [21:25–30](https://ref.ly/Luke21:25-Luke21:30).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 21:32 (id: 20800)

this generation will not pass from the scene: This saying is puzzling—Jesus seems to have predicted that he would come again before the first generation of his disciples died out. There are three main interpretations: (1\) the reference is to the destruction of Jerusalem in AD 70 when Jesus “came” in judgment against the nation of Israel; (2\) “this generation” is not Jesus’ contemporaries, but a future generation alive when these signs begin coming to fulfillment; (3\) the term *generation* could be translated “race,” referring to the Jewish race that will not disappear before Christ returns.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`)

## Luke 21:33 (id: 20801)

*Heaven and earth will pass away*: See study note on 16:17.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:33
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 16:17 (ID: `20618@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 21:34 (id: 20802)

The main theme of this teaching is, “Watch out!” Believers must always stay alert and be ready for Christ’s return.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Everyday Matters (ID: `keyterm:OfThisLife`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 21:36 (id: 20803)

*to stand before*: This means to "pass the test" and stand approved before Jesus Christ at his second coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 22:1 (id: 20818)

The Passover meal was held on the 15th of Nisan (March\~April) in the Jewish calendar and was immediately followed by the seven\-day Festival of Unleavened Bread ([Exod 12:1–20](https://ref.ly/Exod12:1-Exod12:20); [23:15](https://ref.ly/Exod23:15); [34:18](https://ref.ly/Exod34:18); [Deut 16:1–8](https://ref.ly/Deut16:1-Deut16:8)), sometimes also called Passover.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`)

## Luke 22:1–6 (id: 20810)

The religious leaders who had been plotting against Jesus (see [19:47](https://ref.ly/Luke19:47)) found their opportunity when Judas Iscariot approached them with an offer to betray Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`)

## Luke 22:3 (id: 20834)

Satan entered into Judas Iscariot: See [John 13:2](https://ref.ly/John13:2); [Acts 5:3](https://ref.ly/Acts5:3). Satan had previously tempted Jesus ([Luke 4:2](https://ref.ly/Luke4:2)) and had been waiting for this opportunity ([4:13](https://ref.ly/Luke4:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`)

## Luke 22:4 (id: 20840)

*temple officers*: These were leaders within the temple’s own security force. The temple had its own police force to keep order and protect the sacred area.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:4


## Luke 22:5 (id: 20845)

Judas wanted the *money*. Judas wanted the money. John says that he was a thief and used to steal from the money bag of the disciples ([John 12:6](https://ref.ly/John12:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:5


## Luke 22:6 (id: 20852)

*in the absence of a crowd*: They waited until the crowds were not present. They did this because Jesus was very popular with the people.

The leaders were afraid of how the crowd might react if they arrested him in public.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:6


## Luke 22:7 (id: 20856)

The Passover lamb was killed at twilight (the time between sunset and darkness). The Passover meal was then eaten that same evening (see [Exodus 12:6–8](https://ref.ly/Exod12:6-Exod12:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Luke 22:7–30 (id: 20853)

Jesus celebrated the traditional Jewish Passover but transformed it with reference to his own sacrificial death as the Passover lamb. He also prepared his disciples for his coming death and the leadership role they would assume over his church.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:7-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Guest Room (ID: `realia:GuestRoom`); Upstairs Room (ID: `realia:UpstairsRoom`); Clay Jar (ID: `realia:ClayJar`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 22:8 (id: 20857)

Go and prepare the Passover meal: The lamb was roasted and eaten with bitter herbs and unleavened bread at the Passover meal ([Exod 12:8](https://ref.ly/Exod12:8); [Num 9:11–12](https://ref.ly/Num9:11-Num9:12); [Deut 16:3](https://ref.ly/Deut16:3)). The bitter herbs represented the bitterness of the Israelites’ slavery in Egypt, and the unleavened bread recalled their haste in leaving Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); John (ID: `person:John.2`)

## Luke 22:10 (id: 20811)

*a man carrying a jug of water*: This was unusual because women usually carried the water.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** House (ID: `realia:House`); Clay Jar (ID: `realia:ClayJar`)

## Luke 22:11–12 (id: 20812)

The text does not say whether Jesus knew about the room because of his divine nature or if he had made arrangements for this room ahead of time (see also [19:30–34](https://ref.ly/Luke19:30-Luke19:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Guest Room (ID: `realia:GuestRoom`); Upstairs Room (ID: `realia:UpstairsRoom`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Luke 22:14 (id: 20813)

*Jesus reclined at the table*: The Jewish people adopted the Greek custom of reclining on couches next to tables at banquets (see study note on John 13:23).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:14
* **Associated Articles:** John 13:23 (ID: `18909@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 22:15 (id: 20814)

Jesus ate the Passover meal with his disciples and followed the ritual order of the Passover celebration (see study note on Matthew 26:26–29).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:15
* **Associated Articles:** Matthew 26:26-29 (ID: `22048@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Luke 22:16 (id: 20815)

until its meaning is fulfilled in the Kingdom of God: This celebration had as its goal the messianic banquet (see [14:1–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:24)). Jesus’ death as the true Passover lamb ([1 Cor 5:7](https://ref.ly/1Cor5:7)) inaugurated the Kingdom of God that will be completed at Jesus’ second coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 22:17 (id: 20816)

he took a cup of wine: The traditional Passover celebration used four cups of wine—at the opening benediction, after the Passover explanation, after the meal, and after a concluding psalm. This was probably the first cup that introduced the ceremony.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:17


## Luke 22:19–20 (id: 20817)

Using the bread and cup as symbols of his body and blood, Jesus instituted the communion service that the disciples were to practice in remembrance of his death. • This is my body, which is given for you: The unleavened bread of the Passover symbolized Jesus’ death as the Passover lamb. His death would pay for the sins of the world (cp. [1 Cor 11:24–25](https://ref.ly/1Cor11:24-1Cor11:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`)

## Luke 22:20 (id: 20819)

God’s covenants in the Old Testament were confirmed with the blood of a sacrifice ([Gen 15:9–10](https://ref.ly/Gen15:9-Gen15:10); [Exod 24:8](https://ref.ly/Exod24:8)). Jesus’ death established the new covenant that God had promised his people ([Jer 31:31–34](https://ref.ly/Jer31:31-Jer31:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Luke 22:21–22 (id: 20820)

*it has been determined*: Wicked men betrayed and killed Jesus, but this took place as part of God’s purposeful plan to bring salvation (see [Acts 2:23](https://ref.ly/Acts2:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`)

## Luke 22:22 (id: 20821)

*woe to that man who betrays Him*: Judas suffered guilt, judgment, and a terrible death for betraying Jesus ([Matthew 27:5](https://ref.ly/Matt27:5); [Acts 1:18](https://ref.ly/Acts1:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`)

## Luke 22:24 (id: 20822)

*which of them should be considered the greatest*: See also [Mark 9:33–37](https://ref.ly/Mark9:33-Mark9:37) and [10:35–45](https://ref.ly/Mark10:35-Mark10:45).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:24


## Luke 22:25 (id: 20823)

*benefactors*: This translates a Greek word that refers to the practice of rulers bestowing gifts and favors on their subjects to gain loyalty and honor. Jesus contrasted this worldly style of leadership (military power, coercion, and bribery) with his own servant leadership in giving himself for others.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`)

## Luke 22:27 (id: 20824)

For I am among you as one who serves: Jesus defined true leadership as service—meeting the needs of others and empowering them to be all that God has called them to be (see [Mark 10:45](https://ref.ly/Mark10:45)). This statement was striking in a culture for which status and power were central.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`)

## Luke 22:29–30 (id: 20825)

*you may eat and drink at My table*: See “The Messianic Banquet” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:29-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Luke 22:30 (id: 20826)

you will sit on thrones, judging the twelve tribes of Israel: This could be a figurative reference to the apostles’ leadership in the church, or it could indicate their special role in the future when the Kingdom would be consummated on earth (cp. [Eph 2:20](https://ref.ly/Eph2:20); [Rev 3:21](https://ref.ly/Rev3:21); [20:4](https://ref.ly/Rev20:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Luke 22:31 (id: 20827)

Regarding Satan, see [4:2](https://ref.ly/Luke4:2); [10:18](https://ref.ly/Luke10:18); [11:15](https://ref.ly/Luke11:15). Satan is the accuser of God’s people (see [Job 1:7](https://ref.ly/Job1:7); [2:2](https://ref.ly/Job2:2)); he would test Simon Peter and the rest of the apostles. • asked to sift: Wheat was sifted through a sieve to separate the grain from the chaff. It is an image of extreme testing (see [Isa 30:28](https://ref.ly/Isa30:28); [Amos 9:9](https://ref.ly/Amos9:9)). • The NLT’s each of you is precise, because the Greek *you* is plural, referring to all the disciples. Jesus foresaw Peter’s denial and the trials of the other disciples.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 22:32 (id: 20828)

*when you have turned back*: The *you* here is singular and refers to Peter's restoration after he denied Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Luke 22:33 (id: 20829)

See [Acts 4:3](https://ref.ly/Acts4:3); [5:18](https://ref.ly/Acts5:18); [12:1–9](https://ref.ly/Acts12:1-Acts12:9), where Peter suffered trials and was repeatedly jailed. Church tradition relates that Peter was crucified in Rome, but his declaration of loyalty here was premature.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Luke 22:34 (id: 20830)

On Thursday evening, Jesus said that Peter would deny him before the rooster crows tomorrow morning (literally *before the rooster crows today*): This does not contradict Mark’s “this very night, before the rooster crows twice” ([Mark 14:30](https://ref.ly/Mark14:30)); Luke and Matthew are simply more general, and in Jewish reckoning, sunset was the beginning of the next day.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`)

## Luke 22:36 (id: 20831)

take your money and a traveler’s bag: For their missions to Israel’s villages, the apostles had traveled without provisions and depended on others’ support ([9:3](https://ref.ly/Luke9:3); [10:3–4](https://ref.ly/Luke10:3-Luke10:4)). In the dangerous days to come, they would have to plan more carefully. Jesus prepared his disciples for his suffering and death, and also for the establishing of the church and the worldwide proclamation of the Good News. • a sword: Jesus was probably not telling his followers to take up arms, but was speaking metaphorically—they must prepare themselves for a violent crisis.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`)

## Luke 22:38 (id: 20832)

That’s enough: The disciples’ two swords were probably not enough to defend the whole group, and Jesus elsewhere rejected violence ([22:49–51](https://ref.ly/Luke22:49-Luke22:51) *//* [Matt 26:52](https://ref.ly/Matt26:52)). Jesus might have meant that two swords were sufficient to fulfill [Isa 53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa53:12). Or perhaps he meant, “Enough of this foolish talk—you are misunderstanding me.”

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`)

## Luke 22:39 (id: 20833)

*Mount of Olives*: See study note on 19:29. This was the location of the garden of Gethsemane (see [Matthew 26:36](https://ref.ly/Matt26:36); [Mark 14:32](https://ref.ly/Mark14:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:39
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 19:29 (ID: `20697@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`)

## Luke 22:42 (id: 20835)

*take this cup from Me*: A cup is a metaphor for experiencing either judgment or blessing (see, for example, [Psalms 23:5](https://ref.ly/Ps23:5); [75:8](https://ref.ly/Ps75:8); [116:13](https://ref.ly/Ps116:13); [Isaiah 51:17](https://ref.ly/Isa51:17)). Jesus felt all the emotions of his humanity, including fear and anxiety.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 22:43 (id: 20836)

*an angel from heaven*: Angels often help and encourage human beings (see [Hebrews 1:14](https://ref.ly/Heb1:14); compare [1 Kings 19:5–8](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:5-1Kgs19:8); [Psalm 91:11–12](https://ref.ly/Ps91:11-Ps91:12); [Daniel 3:28](https://ref.ly/Dan3:28); [10:16–19](https://ref.ly/Dan10:16-Dan10:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 22:44 (id: 20837)

*His sweat became like drops of blood falling to the ground*: The text does not say that Jesus sweated blood, as people often think. It says that his sweat fell to the ground like blood. This probably means that, in his agony, he was sweating very heavily.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Luke 22:45–46 (id: 20838)

Jesus prayed in great distress, while the disciples failed to remain alert against temptation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:45-46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 22:47 (id: 20839)

A kiss was a common way for friends to greet each other. Judas’s kiss made his betrayal even more clear.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`)

## Luke 22:51 (id: 20841)

he touched the man’s ear and healed him: John tells us that the man’s name was Malchus and that Peter was the one who cut off his ear ([John 18:10](https://ref.ly/John18:10)). Peter probably thought that he was precipitating the final war and that the heavenly host was about to appear and destroy the Romans. When Jesus refused to fight, the terror\-stricken disciples fled ([Matt 26:56](https://ref.ly/Matt26:56) *//* [Mark 14:50](https://ref.ly/Mark14:50)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 22:52 (id: 20842)

The Greek word translated revolutionary normally means “thief,” but the Romans used this term for violent revolutionaries who opposed Roman authority (such as Barabbas, [23:18–19](https://ref.ly/Luke23:18-Luke23:19)). To patriotic Jews, they were freedom fighters; to the Romans, they were common thugs.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 22:54 (id: 20843)

The high priest’s home might have been the home of Caiaphas, the current high priest, or Annas, his father\-in\-law and a previous high priest (see study note on 3:2; [John 18:12–14](https://ref.ly/John18:12-John18:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:54
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:2 (ID: `20939@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 22:59 (id: 20844)

*for he too is a Galilean*: Peter’s way of speaking made it clear that he was from Galilee (compare [Judges 12:5–6](https://ref.ly/Judg12:5-Judg12:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:59

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Luke 22:61 (id: 20846)

*the Lord turned and looked at Peter*: At that time, Jesus was inside the house, where the leaders were questioning him. He may have looked through a window or an open entryway (a covered space at the entrance of a house).

When their eyes met, the rooster crowed. Then Peter remembered what Jesus had said earlier, that Peter would deny him ([22:34](https://ref.ly/Luke22:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:61

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`)

## Luke 22:63 (id: 20847)

*began to mock Him and beat Him*: See [18:32](https://ref.ly/Luke18:32); [Isaiah 50:6](https://ref.ly/Isa50:6); [53:3–5](https://ref.ly/Isa53:3-Isa53:5).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:63


## Luke 22:64 (id: 20848)

*Prophesy! Who hit You?*: Jesus had a reputation as a prophet ([4:24](https://ref.ly/Luke4:24); [7:16](https://ref.ly/Luke7:16), [39](https://ref.ly/Luke7:39); [13:33](https://ref.ly/Luke13:33); [24:19](https://ref.ly/Luke24:19)), so the soldiers mocked this claim.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:64

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`)

## Luke 22:66 (id: 20849)

At daybreak: It was Friday morning (see study note on 22:34). • all the elders of the people assembled: The Jewish high council (Greek *Sanhedrin*) was originally made up of the Jerusalem nobility, both civil leaders and priests, with the high priest as its head. In Jesus’ time, the Sadducees still controlled the Sanhedrin ([Acts 5:17](https://ref.ly/Acts5:17)), though leading Pharisees had also gained a prominent place on the council ([Acts 23:7](https://ref.ly/Acts23:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:66
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 22:34 (ID: `20830@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 22:67–68 (id: 20850)

If I tell you, you won’t believe me: Jesus pointed out the hypocrisy of the question. They had already made up their minds that he was not the Messiah, so it would do no good to answer.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:67-68

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Luke 22:69 (id: 20851)

Jesus referred to [Ps 110:1](https://ref.ly/Ps110:1) (cp. [Luke 20:42–43](https://ref.ly/Luke20:42-Luke20:43)). Though he would be crucified, Jesus would be vindicated through his resurrection and exalted to God’s right hand (see [Acts 2:33–36](https://ref.ly/Acts2:33-Acts2:36); cp. [Dan 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14)). Furthermore, he would be seated in the place of power as Judge. Jesus would soon sit in judgment over those who were trying him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:69

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 22:70 (id: 20854)

The significance of Jesus’ statement ([22:69](https://ref.ly/Luke22:69)) was not lost on the council members. The Messiah was expected to be the Son of God and have a special father–son relationship with God (see study notes on 1:32–33; 4:41; cp. [2 Sam 7:14](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:14); [Pss 2:7](https://ref.ly/Ps2:7); [89:26](https://ref.ly/Ps89:26)). The Sanhedrin accused Jesus of claiming to be the Messiah (cp. study notes on Mark 14:62, 63–64). • You say that I am: This is a qualified affirmation. Jesus probably meant, “What you say is true, but you do not understand what ‘Messiah’ means.” They understood it as a confirmation ([Luke 22:71](https://ref.ly/Luke22:71)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:70
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:32-33 (ID: `20431@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 4:41 (ID: `20969@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Mark 14:62 (ID: `21382@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Mark 14:63-64 (ID: `21383@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elders (ID: `group:GenericElders`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Priests (ID: `group:GenericPriests`)

## Luke 22:71 (id: 20855)

Why do we need other witnesses? The high council regarded Jesus’ confirmation that he was both the Messiah and the Son of God as a blasphemous claim and thus as proof of his guilt.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:71

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elders (ID: `group:GenericElders`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); Priests (ID: `group:GenericPriests`)

## Luke 23:1 (id: 20862)

The council did not have the right to administer capital punishment ([John 18:31](https://ref.ly/John18:31)), so they had to take Jesus before the Roman governor*,* who was in Jerusalem to maintain order during the potentially turbulent Passover festival.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`)

## Luke 23:2 (id: 20867)

The religious charges against Jesus were now replaced by political ones in order to gain a Roman conviction. Pilate was only interested in what concerned Rome. The Jewish charge of blasphemy would not be sufficient, so the council had to show that Jesus was a danger to Rome. They accused Jesus of inciting insurrection against Roman taxation and claiming to be a king. All but the last charge were false.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tribute Tax (ID: `keyterm:TributeTax`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`)

## Luke 23:3 (id: 20875)

You have said it: This is the same vague answer that Jesus gave to the high council ([22:70](https://ref.ly/Luke22:70)), emphasizing Jesus’ acceptance of the title “king of the Jews” but suggesting that Pilate’s understanding of the title was different from his own.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)

## Luke 23:4 (id: 20883)

*I find no basis for a charge against this man*: Jesus did not appear to be a political threat to Pilate.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`)

## Luke 23:5 (id: 20889)

*He stirs up the people*: This accusation was also false, but the religious leaders knew that Pilate feared civil unrest and revolt against Rome.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`)

## Luke 23:6–7 (id: 20890)

Pilate realized that Jesus was from Galilee and was under the authority of Herod Antipas. This gave Pilate a way to avoid the situation without condemning an innocent man or upsetting the religious leaders.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 23:8 (id: 20891)

*he was greatly pleased*: He was curious about Jesus and his power to do miracles and teach, just as he had been curious about John the Baptist ([9:7](https://ref.ly/Luke9:7), [9](https://ref.ly/Luke9:9); [Mark 6:20](https://ref.ly/Mark6:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`)

## Luke 23:9 (id: 20892)

*He gave no answer*: See [Isaiah 53:7](https://ref.ly/Isa53:7).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:9


## Luke 23:12 (id: 20858)

*they had been enemies*: There was a history of conflict between Herod Antipas and Pilate. Herod and three of his brothers had earlier accused Pilate before Tiberius Caesar. This happened when Pilate placed gold shields with pagan symbols in Jerusalem, and Tiberius ordered him to remove them.

Pilate also ruled Judea, a region that Herod Antipas’s father, Herod the Great, had once ruled. Antipas hoped that he might one day rule this area himself.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`)

## Luke 23:14–15 (id: 20859)

Pilate and Herod repeatedly declared Jesus innocent ([23:4](https://ref.ly/Luke23:4), [22](https://ref.ly/Luke23:22))—Jesus was the righteous and innocent suffering servant of the Lord (see also [23:41](https://ref.ly/Luke23:41), [47](https://ref.ly/Luke23:47); [Isa 53:11](https://ref.ly/Isa53:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Discern (ID: `keyterm:Discern`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`)

## Luke 23:16–18 (id: 20860)

I will have him flogged (or *I will teach him a lesson!*): The Greek word can mean “instruct,” “punish,” or “discipline”; it refers to a relatively mild whipping given for lesser offenses. It was different from the severe flogging that Romans gave in preparation for crucifixion (see [Matt 27:26](https://ref.ly/Matt27:26); [Mark 15:15](https://ref.ly/Mark15:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:16-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Barabbas (ID: `person:Barabbas`)

## Luke 23:19 (id: 20861)

Palestine was a breeding ground for insurrection in the decades leading up to the Jewish revolt of AD 66–74\. The Jewish historian Josephus describes a series of first\-century revolutionaries, such as Judas of Galilee, who opposed Roman rule and taxation (see also [Acts 5:36–37](https://ref.ly/Acts5:36-Acts5:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Luke 23:24 (id: 20863)

So Pilate sentenced Jesus to die: Pilate, always the ruthless pragmatist, felt that it was worth a miscarriage of justice to avoid antagonizing the religious leaders and crowds and jeopardizing his political career (see [John 19:12](https://ref.ly/John19:12) and corresponding study note).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:24
* **Associated Articles:** John 19:12 (ID: `19023@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`)

## Luke 23:26 (id: 20864)

Simon might have been a Jewish pilgrim visiting Jerusalem for Passover. • put the cross on him: Prisoners bound for crucifixion were normally forced to carry the crossbeam to the place of execution. It was fastened to the upright beam at the crucifixion site.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cyrene (ID: `place:Cyrene`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.5`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 23:28 (id: 20865)

Jesus warned about the terrible things that would happen to Jerusalem during its siege and destruction in AD 70\.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 23:29 (id: 20866)

Fortunate indeed are the women who are childless: Childlessness was normally a cause of great shame (see study note on 1:7), but during this catastrophe, those who didn’t have to see their children starve to death would be the fortunate ones.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:29
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:7 (ID: `20452@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`)

## Luke 23:30 (id: 20868)

*‘they will say to the mountains, “Fall on us!”‘*: This refers to a time when people would prefer death over the terrible suffering they would face. Jesus warned that Jerusalem would experience such terrible pain and destruction that people would beg for mountains to fall on them to end their suffering quickly.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:30


## Luke 23:31 (id: 20869)

If the Romans crucified an innocent man when the tree was green (i.e., during a period of relative peace), what more horrible things would they do when it was dry (i.e., when the land had become a tinderbox of revolution)? During the siege of Jerusalem (AD 66–70\), the Romans crucified thousands of Jews outside the city of Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:31


## Luke 23:33 (id: 20870)

The Skull: Greek kranion; the Aramaic term is *Golgotha* ([Matt 27:33](https://ref.ly/Matt27:33); [Mark 15:22](https://ref.ly/Mark15:22)). The location is uncertain, but it has traditionally been associated with the present Church of the Holy Sepulchre. A less likely location is Gordon’s Calvary, a rock outcrop near the Garden Tomb. • they nailed him to the cross: Victims were often tied to the cross or nailed through the wrists and ankles to hasten death—here, the bodies had to be buried before sunset due to the approaching Sabbath.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`); Golgotha (ID: `place:Golgotha`)

## Luke 23:34 (id: 20871)

*by casting lots* (see [John 19:23–24](https://ref.ly/John19:23-John19:24)): The prisoner’s possessions were treated as spoils of war and divided among the soldiers.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Lot (ID: `realia:Lot`)

## Luke 23:35 (id: 20872)

The crowd watched and the leaders scoffed (see [Ps 22:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps22:7-Ps22:8)): [Psalm 22](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1-Ps22:31) speaks of David, a righteous sufferer, crying out to God for protection. Jesus, David’s descendant, was the ultimate righteous sufferer who fulfilled [Ps 22](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1-Ps22:31).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 23:36 (id: 20873)

*sour wine*: This was a popular drink of the lower classes, especially among soldiers (compare [Psalm 69:21](https://ref.ly/Ps69:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sour Wine (ID: `realia:SourWine`)

## Luke 23:38 (id: 20874)

The sign confirmed that Jesus was crucified on the charge of claiming to be the King of the Jews (compare [John 19:19–22](https://ref.ly/John19:19-John19:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)

## Luke 23:42 (id: 20876)

*remember me when You come into Your kingdom!*: The criminal was perhaps thinking of the resurrection at the end of time, when Jesus would be raised up and vindicated by God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 23:43 (id: 20877)

Jesus corrected the man by assuring him that today he would be vindicated in God’s presence (see also [2:11](https://ref.ly/Luke2:11); [4:21](https://ref.ly/Luke4:21); [5:26](https://ref.ly/Luke5:26); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Luke19:9)). • The word translated paradise comes from a Persian word that means “garden”; it was used in Jewish literature for the Garden of Eden and also for heaven, the place of eternal bliss for God’s people (see [2 Cor 12:4](https://ref.ly/2Cor12:4); [Rev 2:7](https://ref.ly/Rev2:7); cp. [Luke 16:22–23](https://ref.ly/Luke16:22-Luke16:23); [2 Cor 5:8](https://ref.ly/2Cor5:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Paradise (ID: `keyterm:Paradise`)

## Luke 23:44 (id: 20878)

Darkness symbolizes sorrow as well as God’s judgment ([Psalm 23:4](https://ref.ly/Ps23:4); [Isaiah 8:22](https://ref.ly/Isa8:22); [9:1–2](https://ref.ly/Isa9:1-Isa9:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:44


## Luke 23:45 (id: 20879)

the curtain in the sanctuary of the Temple: One curtain separated the Temple courtyard from the Holy Place, and another separated the Holy Place from the Most Holy Place. Luke does not say which was torn down the middle*,* but it was probably the latter—Jesus’ death put an end to the sacrificial system of the Temple and opened a new and permanent way into the presence of God (see [Heb 10:19–20](https://ref.ly/Heb10:19-Heb10:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 23:46 (id: 20880)

Father, I entrust my spirit into your hands! See [Ps 31:5](https://ref.ly/Ps31:5); [Ps 31](https://ref.ly/Ps31:1-Ps31:24) is another psalm about a righteous sufferer (see study note on Luke 23:35).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:46
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 23:35 (ID: `20872@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 23:47 (id: 20881)

innocent (or *righteous;* cp. [Mark 15:39](https://ref.ly/Mark15:39)): Cp. [Luke 23:14–15](https://ref.ly/Luke23:14-Luke23:15). The innocent and righteous servant of the Lord died as a sacrifice for the sins of his people ([Isa 53:11](https://ref.ly/Isa53:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Luke 23:48 (id: 20882)

*they returned home beating their breasts*: This was a sign of sorrow and mourning.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:48


## Luke 23:50–51 (id: 20884)

* Not all the religious leaders opposed Jesus. Joseph had disagreed with the decision of the council.
* The exact location of Arimathea in Judea is unknown. It may have been Ramathaim, or Ramah, about 32 kilometers (20 miles) northwest of Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:50-51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Arimathea (ID: `place:Arimathea`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.6`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 23:52 (id: 20885)

*He went to Pilate to ask for the body of Jesus*: The Romans used crucifixion as a public warning against revolt, so they did not generally allow a victim to be buried. Instead, they left the body exposed or rotting on the cross. An exception was made in this case, probably because Pilate knew that Jesus was innocent and because of Joseph’s position of honor on the Jewish high council.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`)

## Luke 23:53 (id: 20886)

The new tomb had probably been purchased by Joseph as a family tomb.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Luke 23:54 (id: 20887)

* *Preparation Day*: This happened late on Friday afternoon. The Greek text does not specifically mention Friday. It refers to the day Jews prepare for the Sabbath day ceremonies during Passover week. The Sabbath day begins on Friday evening.
* As the Sabbath was about to begin, they had to bury the body quickly. The Jews could not work during the Sabbath, which began at sunset.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:54

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`)

## Luke 23:55–56 (id: 20888)

The women took special note of the tomb’s location because they would return with spices and ointments to anoint his body. The spices were to mask the stench of a rotting corpse. A body would be placed on a stone bench in the side of the tomb until the flesh decomposed. The bones would then be collected and placed in a small ossuary, or bone box, and placed on a shelf in the tomb. In this way, a tomb could be used for many family members over an extended period of time.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:55-56

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aloes (ID: `realia:Aloes`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Myrrh (ID: `realia:Myrrh`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Myrrh (ID: `flora:Myrrh`)

## Luke 24:1 (id: 20901)

But very early on Sunday morning: The early church worshiped on the first day of the week (Sunday) instead of the seventh (Saturday, the Sabbath) because Jesus rose on a Sunday morning (cp. [Acts 20:7](https://ref.ly/Acts20:7); [Rev 1:10](https://ref.ly/Rev1:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Week (ID: `keyterm:Week`); Aloes (ID: `realia:Aloes`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 24:1–12 (id: 20893)

Women were the first to find the tomb empty and to hear the announcement of the resurrection. This is strong evidence for the historicity of the resurrection accounts. In first\-century Judaism, women were not regarded as reliable witnesses, so the church would never have created stories in which women discovered the empty tomb.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:1-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Joanna (ID: `person:Joanna`); Week (ID: `keyterm:Week`); James (ID: `person:James.3`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Disbelieve (ID: `keyterm:Disbelieve`); Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Mary Magdalene (ID: `person:Marymagdalene`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`); Aloes (ID: `realia:Aloes`)

## Luke 24:2 (id: 20907)

The stone was probably a large round stone that was rolled down a track to cover the tomb’s entrance.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 24:4 (id: 20917)

two men suddenly appeared: Angels often appear as young men in Scripture ([Gen 18:2](https://ref.ly/Gen18:2); [19:1](https://ref.ly/Gen19:1), [10](https://ref.ly/Gen19:10); [Josh 5:13](https://ref.ly/Josh5:13); [Judg 13:6–11](https://ref.ly/Judg13:6-Judg13:11); [Heb 13:2](https://ref.ly/Heb13:2)). Dazzling white robes symbolize purity and holiness ([Dan 10:5–6](https://ref.ly/Dan10:5-Dan10:6); [Acts 1:10](https://ref.ly/Acts1:10); [Rev 4:4](https://ref.ly/Rev4:4); [19:14](https://ref.ly/Rev19:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 24:5–7 (id: 20918)

Jesus had told his followers many times that he would rise from the dead (see [9:22](https://ref.ly/Luke9:22), [44](https://ref.ly/Luke9:44); [17:25](https://ref.ly/Luke17:25); [18:31–34](https://ref.ly/Luke18:31-Luke18:34)). Jesus’ resurrection is a key tenet of the Christian faith (see [1 Cor 15:3–7](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:3-1Cor15:7), [12–57](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:12-1Cor15:57)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 24:7 (id: 20923)

Jesus rose on the third day, counting Friday as the first day and Sunday as the third.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 24:9 (id: 20924)

*the Eleven*: These were the Twelve disciples minus Judas. Judas Iscariot had betrayed Jesus and was no longer with them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

Mary Magdalene and Joanna are mentioned in [8:2–3](https://ref.ly/Luke8:2-Luke8:3). Mary the mother of James is not the mother of James, the son of Zebedee (see [Matt 27:56](https://ref.ly/Matt27:56)), but might be the mother of James, the son of Alphaeus ([Luke 6:15](https://ref.ly/Luke6:15)).

*Peter* … *ran to the tomb*: John went with him ([John 20:2–9](https://ref.ly/John20:2-John20:9)).

## Luke 24:13 (id: 20897)

two of Jesus’ followers: Jesus had many disciples in addition to the Twelve (see [10:1](https://ref.ly/Luke10:1), [17](https://ref.ly/Luke10:17)). Nothing else is known of Cleopas ([24:18](https://ref.ly/Luke24:18)) or the other disciple (who may have been his wife). They were probably returning from celebrating the Passover in Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:13

## Luke 24:13–34 (id: 20896)

This account is Luke’s unique contribution to the resurrection narratives. Jesus corrected these disciples’ misunderstanding by showing from Scripture that it was necessary for the Messiah to suffer. He then opened their eyes to the truth of his resurrection.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:13-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Written (ID: `keyterm:Scripture`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Cleopas (ID: `person:Cleopas`); Emmaus (ID: `place:Emmaus`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Liberation (ID: `keyterm:Liberation`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Stade (ID: `realia:Stade`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 24:16 (id: 20898)

God kept them from recognizing him: The Greek uses a passive construction (*they were kept from recognizing him*) known as a “divine passive,” with God as the implied subject. God intentionally blinded their eyes until the moment of revelation, when Jesus broke bread with them ([24:30–31](https://ref.ly/Luke24:30-Luke24:31); cp. [2 Kgs 6:17](https://ref.ly/2Kgs6:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:16


## Luke 24:18 (id: 20899)

the only person in Jerusalem who hasn’t heard about all the things: The Romans conducted crucifixions at major public crossroads to make an example of the victims and warn others against revolt. It was unthinkable to these disciples that a Passover pilgrim would not have heard of Jesus’ crucifixion.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cleopas (ID: `person:Cleopas`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 24:19 (id: 20900)

Jesus is often portrayed as a prophet in Luke’s Gospel (see [7:16](https://ref.ly/Luke7:16); compare with the description of Moses in [Acts 7:22](https://ref.ly/Acts7:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 24:21 (id: 20902)

*we were hoping He was the One who would redeem Israel*: This refers to the Messiah. The crucifixion had ended their hopes.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Liberation (ID: `keyterm:Liberation`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`)

## Luke 24:22 (id: 20903)

*some of our women astounded us*: See [24:10–11](https://ref.ly/Luke24:10-Luke24:11).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 24:25–26 (id: 20904)

the prophets wrote . . . that the Messiah would have to suffer: The crucifixion did not negate Jesus’ identity as the Messiah but confirmed it, because the death of the Messiah was predicted in Scripture. Jesus rebuked the two for not recognizing from Scripture that the Messiah had to suffer (see [Isa 50:4–9](https://ref.ly/Isa50:4-Isa50:9); [52:13–53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:13-Isa53:12); [Pss 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12), [16](https://ref.ly/Ps16:1-Ps16:11), [22](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1-Ps22:31), [118](https://ref.ly/Ps118:1-Ps118:29); cp. [Luke 18:32](https://ref.ly/Luke18:32); [20:17](https://ref.ly/Luke20:17); [23:37](https://ref.ly/Luke23:37); [Acts 2:25–28](https://ref.ly/Acts2:25-Acts2:28); [4:25–26](https://ref.ly/Acts4:25-Acts4:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:25-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Luke 24:27 (id: 20905)

All the Scriptures*,* meaning all of the Old Testament, point forward to the coming of Jesus the Messiah and the salvation that he would bring.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Written (ID: `keyterm:Scripture`)

## Luke 24:29 (id: 20906)

they begged him, “Stay the night with us”: Middle Eastern hospitality demanded that they offer food and lodging to this stranger ([Gen 18:3](https://ref.ly/Gen18:3); [19:2](https://ref.ly/Gen19:2); [Judg 19:5–9](https://ref.ly/Judg19:5-Judg19:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:29


## Luke 24:30 (id: 20908)

As they sat down to eat: The meal is a symbol of the messianic banquet and of the salvation that God is accomplishing ([Isa 25:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:6-Isa25:8); [Luke 14:1–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:24)). • Then he broke it and gave it to them: This action recalls the feeding of the 5,000 ([9:16](https://ref.ly/Luke9:16)) and the institution of the Lord’s Supper ([22:19](https://ref.ly/Luke22:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Luke 24:31 (id: 20909)

their eyes were opened: This is another example of the “divine passive” (see study note on 24:16); God opened their eyes at the breaking of the bread. • at that moment he disappeared! In his resurrected and transformed body, Jesus had abilities not realized before the resurrection (see [24:36](https://ref.ly/Luke24:36); [1 Cor 15:20–23](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:20-1Cor15:23), [35–57](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:35-1Cor15:57)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:31
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 24:16 (ID: `20898@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 24:34 (id: 20910)

*Simon*: The Gospels do not describe this appearance to Simon Peter, but Paul also confirms it in [1 Corinthians 15:5](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:5).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 24:39 (id: 20911)

*a spirit does not have flesh and bones, as you see I have*: After rising from the dead, Jesus was not a spirit without a body. He had a real body that could never die again or decay. Believers will receive similar bodies at the final resurrection ([1 Corinthians 15:35–57](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:35-1Cor15:57)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`)

## Luke 24:44 (id: 20912)

*in the Law of Moses, the Prophets, and the Psalms*: Jesus referred to the three sections of the Hebrew Scriptures: the Law, the Prophets, and the Writings. The entire Old Testament points to God’s salvation through Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`)

## Luke 24:46 (id: 20913)

*The Christ will suffer and rise from the dead*: See study note on 24:25–26.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:46
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 24:25-26 (ID: `20904@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 24:47 (id: 20914)

r*epentance and forgiveness of sins will be proclaimed to all nations*: See [Isaiah 42:6](https://ref.ly/Isa42:6); [49:6](https://ref.ly/Isa49:6); [Joel 2:28–32](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28-Joel2:32); [Amos 9:11–12](https://ref.ly/Amos9:11-Amos9:12); [Acts 2:17–21](https://ref.ly/Acts2:17-Acts2:21); [13:47](https://ref.ly/Acts13:47); [15:16–18](https://ref.ly/Acts15:16-Acts15:18).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`)

## Luke 24:48 (id: 20915)

The primary role of the apostles in the book of Acts was to be witnesses to the fulfillment of Scripture in the life, death, and resurrection of Jesus (see [Acts 1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`)

## Luke 24:49 (id: 20916)

*I am sending the promise of My Father*: The Old Testament prophesied that God would pour out his Holy Spirit on all people ([Isaiah 32:15](https://ref.ly/Isa32:15); [Jeremiah 31:33](https://ref.ly/Jer31:33); [Ezekiel 36:26–27](https://ref.ly/Ezek36:26-Ezek36:27); [Joel 2:28–32](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28-Joel2:32)). This prophecy came true on the day of Pentecost ([Acts 2:14–21](https://ref.ly/Acts2:14-Acts2:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Promise (ID: `keyterm:Promise`); Height (ID: `keyterm:Height`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 24:50 (id: 20920)

*Bethany*: See study note on 19:29. See also [Acts 1:11–12](https://ref.ly/Acts1:11-Acts1:12); [Zechariah 14:4](https://ref.ly/Zech14:4).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:50
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 19:29 (ID: `20697@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethany (ID: `place:Bethany`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Luke 24:50–53 (id: 20919)

Luke also narrates Jesus’ ascension in [Acts 1:1–11](https://ref.ly/Acts1:1-Acts1:11). For Luke, Jesus’ ascension confirmed his vindication, exaltation, and enthronement at God’s right hand as Messiah and Lord ([Acts 2:24–36](https://ref.ly/Acts2:24-Acts2:36)). From his position of glory and authority in heaven, Jesus guides and directs his church.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:50-53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Bethany (ID: `place:Bethany`); Bow Down (ID: `keyterm:BowDown`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 24:51 (id: 20921)

Cp. [2 Kgs 2:11](https://ref.ly/2Kgs2:11). Jesus’ ascension was his exaltation to a position of authority over all creation (see [Acts 2:32–36](https://ref.ly/Acts2:32-Acts2:36); [5:31](https://ref.ly/Acts5:31); [Eph 1:19–23](https://ref.ly/Eph1:19-Eph1:23); [Phil 2:9–11](https://ref.ly/Phil2:9-Phil2:11); [Heb 1:13](https://ref.ly/Heb1:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 24:53 (id: 20922)

The Temple was a place of worship as well as of sacrifice. The apostles continued in their Jewish manner of worship because their goal was not to create a new religion but to announce the fulfillment of Scripture’s promises of salvation through Jesus the Messiah.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
